# All Supabase SQL

Every SQL statement this project **ships** to Supabase, in the order it was
created: the versioned migration set, plus the applier's own bootstrap. One
section per file in `supabase/migrations/`, grouped by the app version that was
current when the phase landed; the text under each label is that migration file
**verbatim**. Two things are deliberately not here, and neither changes the
server: the assertions in `supabase/tests/*.sql` (runnable checks that roll back)
and the read-only counts `tool/check_supabase_seed.dart` and
`tool/apply_supabase_migrations.dart` issue.

- Only one project is ever in scope: `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau` ("Legal Case Diary").
  AGENTS.md rule 12 names the live project that must never be touched.
- Server SQL is written only as a migration file, then this ledger is rebuilt.
  Nothing here is typed by hand, so the two can never disagree.
- Re-running the whole set is safe: creates are `if not exists`, functions are
  `create or replace`, every policy and trigger is preceded by a `drop ... if
  exists`, and all six seed inserts upsert `where <table>.is_user_modified = false`.
- One setting is dashboard-only and is not SQL: Realtime Authorization
  (`supabase/README.md`). pg_cron is installed and the tombstone purge
  scheduled by the last migration — verified on the project.
- Every version here is recorded in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` on
  `lsthasywrdrhuuxnykau`, so `supabase db push` sees the set as applied.

| 0.0.318 | 2026-09-30 | `20260930170000_push_changes_created_at_guard.sql` | `push_changes` again, with one corrected guard: the insert-time `created_at` fallback now fills only an epoch-ms **integer** column, so `conflict_log`'s server-owned `timestamptz` keeps its own default instead of receiving the client's milliseconds (which raised `22007` and made every conflict mirror a permanent rejection — found by running the smoke test with a `conflict_log` row in it). |
| 0.0.324 | 2026-09-30 | `20260930180000_ensure_firm_premium_gate.sql` | `ensure_firm` again, with Task 20 Phase 1's two refusals: the individual -> firm-owner transition now requires a derived premium entitlement (plan §6 — the free plan is strictly individual and single-device; lapsed owners keep the firm they already have), and a member of somebody else's firm can no longer create a second one and relink their profile away from the rows they authored. |
| 0.0.325 | 2026-09-30 | `20260930190000_remove_firm_member.sql` | Task 20 Phase 2's `remove_firm_member(uuid)`: owner-only, `SECURITY DEFINER`, it deletes the membership row and relinks the member's `profiles.firm_id` / `role` to individual — which is what revokes RLS access, Realtime topic authorization and every queued write at once, without touching the auth session (this project has no service-role key). Authored records keep their attribution and are never reassigned; the removal is announced to the removed account's own topic and to the firm's. The same migration attaches the existing broadcast trigger to `firm_members`, so a permission toggle reaches the member it affects. |
| 0.0.327 | 2026-09-30 | `20260930200000_push_changes_delete_review.sql` | Task 20 Phase 4's delete-review branch: `push_changes` replaces whole to teach an approval (a `delete_requests` update setting `status = 'approved'`) what it means server-side. The request is locked (already) and the target is locked next, always in that order so concurrent approvals cannot deadlock; only a member with `can_delete` may review; a target at a different version than the request's `target_base_version` becomes `target_conflict`, parking the request as `stale_review_required` with the version the reviewer just saw so a reconfirm re-decides against the row that exists; an already soft-deleted target becomes `already_resolved` with its tombstone and records no decision; and an unchanged target is tombstoned atomically with its `case_history` cascade and the request resolved beside it, all inside the per-mutation savepoint that already produces idempotent receipts. |
| 0.0.330 | 2026-09-30 | `20260930210000_sync_can_write_firm_membership.sql` | Task 20 audit closure: `sync_can_write` again, so authorship of a firm-scoped row only counts while the row's firm is still the caller's firm (`firm_id = current_firm_id()`). Removing an associate relinks `profiles.firm_id` to NULL, but the author clause checked `user_id = auth.uid()` before any firm helper, so a removed member could still merge a queued **update** to a firm record they happened to author. The narrowing is applied to the same three places that trusted authorship: ordinary rows, custom references, and a requester's own pending delete request. A `firm_id`-less personal row is unaffected, and `can_edit_firm` / `can_delete_firm` still cover teammates and reviewers. |
| 0.0.332 | 2026-09-30 | `20260930220000_rls_firm_membership.sql` | Task 20 audit follow-up: the same membership rule, applied to RLS. `sync_can_write` closed the *app* path in 0.0.330, but the table policies still said `user_id = auth.uid()` on a firm-scoped row, so a removed associate could still write their authored firm rows through PostgREST directly — rewriting one, or detaching it from the firm by nulling `firm_id`. Every INSERT and UPDATE policy that trusts authorship now requires the row to be personal (`firm_id is null`) or still the caller's firm (`is not distinct from current_firm_id()`), across the ten tables that share one shape plus `conflict_log` and the requester's own pending `delete_requests` row. SELECT policies and table privileges are deliberately untouched. |

## Bootstrap — Applier Bookkeeping (before the first apply)

> **Created before the first apply.** Applies to every run of `tool/apply_supabase_migrations.dart`: it creates the migration-history objects once with the Supabase CLI's own DDL, then records one row per applied file through the last statement below. Everything is `if not exists` or `on conflict`, so a project the CLI already linked is left untouched.

```sql
-- Create the migration-history schema.
CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS supabase_migrations;
-- Create the migration-history table.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS supabase_migrations.schema_migrations (version text NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY);
-- Add the statements column (absent on projects whose history predates the CLI column).
ALTER TABLE supabase_migrations.schema_migrations ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS statements text[];
-- Add the name column.
ALTER TABLE supabase_migrations.schema_migrations ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS name text;

-- Run once per migration file, inside that file's transaction,
-- so a file is either fully applied and recorded, or neither.
INSERT INTO supabase_migrations.schema_migrations(version, name, statements) VALUES ('<version>', '<name>', NULL) ON CONFLICT (version) DO UPDATE SET name = EXCLUDED.name, statements = EXCLUDED.statements;
```

## App Version 0.0.289 — Task 16 Phase 1 — Core Schema, Sign-Up Trigger And Reference Seed

### 0.0.289 · `20260929100000_phase1_core_schema.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Creates every Supabase table at once — 12 push/pull domain, 3 pull-only cache, 2 per-user preference and 2 server-managed — mirroring the shipped Drift columns. RLS is switched on in the same statement that creates each table.

```sql
-- Phase 1 (1/2) — Core schema: every Supabase table, created up front so later
-- phases only add behaviour. Column names/types mirror the shipped Drift
-- tables one-for-one (Task 03) so push/pull is lossless; the global sync
-- columns are added only to the push/pull domain class.
--
-- Table classes (AGENTS.md rule 4):
--   push/pull domain  : cases, case_history, notes, case_references,
--                       court_categories, case_categories, courts, steps,
--                       case_types, on_behalfs, delete_requests, conflict_log
--   pull-only cache   : profiles, firms, firm_members
--   per-user pref     : user_preferences, case_notification_preferences
--   server-managed    : firm_invites, payment_submissions
--   (payment_approval_events, mutation_receipts, invite_attempts and app_admins
--    are created in Phase 2.)
--
-- Type conventions (implementation_plan.md, "Timestamp Convention" + Revision
-- 2.4): client display metadata stays BIGINT epoch milliseconds — epoch ms does
-- not fit in a 32-bit INTEGER — while server-authoritative instants are
-- TIMESTAMPTZ. Row keys the client generates (cases, notes, …) are TEXT so a
-- client-supplied UUID string is never rejected; identity/scope columns
-- (user_id, firm_id, approved_by, …) are UUID with FKs to auth.users/firms.
-- Reference-data keys are the stable deterministic seed tags shared by Drift,
-- Supabase and backups (Rev 2.4 §1); they stay TEXT.

-- pgcrypto supplies gen_random_uuid() and, through public.sha256_hex() in
-- Phase 3, the SHA-256 hashing join_firm() needs.
create extension if not exists pgcrypto with schema extensions;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Account & firm scope (pull-only caches on the client)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Firms first: profiles.firm_id and firm_members.firm_id both reference it.
create table if not exists public.firms (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  name text not null,
  owner_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  subscription_status text not null default 'free',
  subscription_expiry timestamptz,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
comment on table public.firms is
  'Pull-only cache. subscription_expiry is written only by mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm() (Phase 3).';

-- One row per authenticated user; created by the sign-up trigger (part 2).
create table if not exists public.profiles (
  id uuid primary key references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  full_name text not null default '',
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  role text not null default 'individual',
  subscription_status text not null default 'free',
  subscription_expiry timestamptz,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
comment on table public.profiles is
  'Pull-only cache. subscription_expiry is the single source of truth for premium entitlement; subscription_status is display-only.';

create table if not exists public.firm_members (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  firm_id uuid not null references public.firms (id) on delete cascade,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  role text not null,
  can_add boolean not null default false,
  can_edit boolean not null default false,
  can_delete boolean not null default false,
  synced_at bigint,
  permissions_synced_at bigint,
  unique (firm_id, user_id)
);
comment on table public.firm_members is
  'Pull-only cache of membership and permission flags; the authoritative permission source for RLS and triggers.';

-- Single-use onboarding codes. Redeemed only inside join_firm() (Phase 3).
create table if not exists public.firm_invites (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  firm_id uuid not null references public.firms (id) on delete cascade,
  code_hash text not null unique,
  created_by uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  used_by uuid references auth.users (id),
  used_at bigint,
  revoked boolean not null default false,
  created_at bigint not null
);
comment on table public.firm_invites is
  'Plaintext codes are never stored; code_hash is sha256 hex of the normalized code (see public.sha256_hex).';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Push/pull domain: editable reference data (stable deterministic TEXT keys)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Global sync columns for every domain table:
--   updated_at       bigint      client edit time, never used for authorization
--   version          integer     server-assigned revision (1 on insert)
--   server_updated_at timestamptz trigger-maintained; the only delta-pull cursor
--   synced_at        bigint      device mirror metadata
--   is_deleted       boolean     soft delete; rows are never hard-deleted
--   last_modified_by text        server-assigned editor id

create table if not exists public.court_categories (
  id text primary key,
  name_key text not null,
  court_level text not null,
  order_index integer not null,
  division text,
  district text,
  is_custom boolean not null default false,
  is_user_modified boolean not null default false,
  user_id uuid references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);
comment on table public.court_categories is
  'Stable machine keys (appellate_division, …). Built-ins are global; custom rows are user- or firm-scoped.';

create table if not exists public.case_categories (
  id text primary key,
  court_category_id text not null references public.court_categories (id),
  name text not null,
  order_index integer not null,
  is_custom boolean not null default false,
  is_user_modified boolean not null default false,
  user_id uuid references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

create table if not exists public.courts (
  id text primary key,
  court_name text not null,
  higher_lower_courts text not null,
  division text,
  district text,
  court_category_id text not null references public.court_categories (id),
  order_index integer not null default 0,
  is_custom boolean not null default false,
  is_user_modified boolean not null default false,
  user_id uuid references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

create table if not exists public.steps (
  id text primary key,
  court_category_id text not null references public.court_categories (id),
  step_name text not null,
  order_index integer not null,
  is_custom boolean not null default false,
  is_user_modified boolean not null default false,
  user_id uuid references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

create table if not exists public.case_types (
  id text primary key,
  court_category_id text not null references public.court_categories (id),
  case_category_id text references public.case_categories (id),
  name text not null,
  order_index integer not null,
  is_custom boolean not null default false,
  is_user_modified boolean not null default false,
  user_id uuid references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

create table if not exists public.on_behalfs (
  id text primary key,
  court_category_id text not null references public.court_categories (id),
  name text not null,
  order_index integer not null,
  is_custom boolean not null default false,
  is_user_modified boolean not null default false,
  user_id uuid references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Push/pull domain: case data
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.cases (
  id text primary key,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  court_id text not null references public.courts (id),
  case_type text not null,
  case_category_id text references public.case_categories (id),
  case_number text not null,
  honorable_judges text,
  tender_number text,
  previous_court_case text,
  district_of_origin text,
  petitioner text not null,
  petitioner_lead_counsel text,
  respondent text not null,
  respondent_lead_counsel text,
  on_behalf_id text not null references public.on_behalfs (id),
  party_phone text,
  case_title_and_section text,
  case_remarks text,
  next_date bigint,
  last_step text,
  notes_results text,
  result text,
  created_at bigint not null,
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);
comment on table public.cases is
  'Mirrors the shipped client model, whose only phone column is party_phone: the represented client''s number (Rev 2.4''s separate petitioner_phone/respondent_phone split was withdrawn by user decision on 2026-09-29, not deferred - do not add those columns).';

-- Immutable hearing timeline. source_mutation_id is the idempotency key of the
-- accepted mutation that created the row; retries cannot duplicate it.
create table if not exists public.case_history (
  id text primary key,
  case_id text not null references public.cases (id) on delete cascade,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  hearing_date bigint not null,
  last_step text,
  next_date bigint,
  note_result text,
  result text,
  created_by uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  source_mutation_id text not null unique,
  base_case_version integer not null,
  result_case_version integer not null,
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

create table if not exists public.notes (
  id text primary key,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  title text not null,
  body text not null,
  items text,
  is_pinned boolean not null default false,
  created_at bigint not null,
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);
comment on column public.notes.items is
  'To-do checklist as a JSON array of {text, done}; null on a plain text note.';

create table if not exists public.case_references (
  id text primary key,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  reference_and_page_no text not null,
  statute text,
  section_or_article text,
  title_and_description text,
  comments_notes text,
  is_pinned boolean not null default false,
  created_at bigint not null,
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

-- Deletion request queue for associates without can_delete.
create table if not exists public.delete_requests (
  id text primary key,
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  entity_type text not null,
  entity_id text not null,
  entity_snapshot text not null,
  target_base_version integer not null,
  requested_by uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  status text not null,
  reviewed_by uuid references auth.users (id),
  review_note text,
  created_at bigint not null,
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);
comment on column public.delete_requests.entity_snapshot is
  'Snapshot of the target at request time, stored as TEXT so a JSON string cannot silently become a JSON string value.';

-- Mirror of the local rejected-mutation log, pulled on a new device for
-- portability. user_id/firm_id are ownership columns the local table does not
-- carry: RLS and the Realtime topic both need them.
create table if not exists public.conflict_log (
  id text primary key,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  firm_id uuid references public.firms (id),
  mutation_id text not null,
  entity_type text not null,
  entity_id text not null,
  base_version integer not null,
  base_payload text not null,
  submitted_payload text not null,
  changed_fields text not null,
  server_version integer not null,
  server_payload text not null,
  resolution_state text not null,
  conflict_at bigint not null,
  resolved_at bigint,
  is_dismissed boolean not null default false,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  updated_at bigint not null,
  version integer not null default 1,
  server_updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  synced_at bigint,
  is_deleted boolean not null default false,
  last_modified_by text
);

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Per-user preferences (never firm-shared)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Server side of the premium cross-device preference sync (Rev 2.4 §4). Rows
-- are self-only under RLS.
create table if not exists public.user_preferences (
  user_id uuid primary key references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  language text,
  theme text,
  view_settings_json text,
  notification_enabled boolean not null default false,
  notification_time bigint,
  notification_default_offsets text,
  updated_at bigint not null
);

create table if not exists public.case_notification_preferences (
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  case_id text not null references public.cases (id) on delete cascade,
  enabled boolean not null default true,
  offsets text,
  time_minutes integer,
  updated_at bigint not null,
  primary key (user_id, case_id)
);

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Server-managed: payment submissions (no Drift counterpart; per the plan)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.payment_submissions (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  plan_type text not null,
  amount integer not null,
  mfs_provider text not null,
  sender_number text not null,
  transaction_id text not null unique,
  status text not null default 'pending',
  admin_notes text,
  created_at bigint not null,
  submitted_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  approved_at timestamptz,
  approved_by uuid references auth.users (id),
  updated_at bigint not null
);
comment on column public.payment_submissions.submitted_at is
  'Authoritative server submission time; the BEFORE INSERT trigger overwrites any client value and the 3-per-24h limit counts it.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Row-level security is switched on with the tables, not later
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Supabase grants `anon` and `authenticated` table privileges by default, so a
-- table is only protected once RLS is on: with RLS on and no policy yet, every
-- client command is denied. Enabling it here means a migration run that stops
-- half-way can never leave a reachable table behind; Phase 4 adds the policies
-- (and re-asserts the same statements, which is idempotent).

alter table public.profiles enable row level security;
alter table public.firms enable row level security;
alter table public.firm_members enable row level security;
alter table public.firm_invites enable row level security;
alter table public.court_categories enable row level security;
alter table public.case_categories enable row level security;
alter table public.courts enable row level security;
alter table public.steps enable row level security;
alter table public.case_types enable row level security;
alter table public.on_behalfs enable row level security;
alter table public.cases enable row level security;
alter table public.case_history enable row level security;
alter table public.notes enable row level security;
alter table public.case_references enable row level security;
alter table public.delete_requests enable row level security;
alter table public.conflict_log enable row level security;
alter table public.user_preferences enable row level security;
alter table public.case_notification_preferences enable row level security;
alter table public.payment_submissions enable row level security;
```

### 0.0.289 · `20260929100100_phase1_signup_profile_trigger.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** handle_new_user() and its on_auth_user_created trigger: each new auth user gets an individual, free, firm-less profiles row. The only INSERT path for profiles.

```sql
-- Phase 1 (2/2) — Sign-up trigger: every new auth.users row gets a profiles row.
--
-- The profile starts as an individual account (`role = 'individual'`,
-- `firm_id = null`, free entitlement) exactly as the plan describes. It is a
-- pull-only cache on the client, so this trigger is the only INSERT path.
--
-- EXECUTE is deliberately NOT revoked from PUBLIC here: PostgreSQL checks the
-- EXECUTE privilege of the role whose statement fires a trigger, and sign-up
-- inserts into auth.users run as the auth service role, so revoking would break
-- account creation. Nothing else is reachable through this function.

create or replace function public.handle_new_user()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  insert into public.profiles (
    id, full_name, role, firm_id,
    subscription_status, subscription_expiry, created_at
  )
  values (
    new.id,
    coalesce(new.raw_user_meta_data ->> 'full_name', ''),
    'individual',
    null,
    'free',
    null,
    now()
  )
  on conflict (id) do nothing;

  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.handle_new_user() is
  'Creates the individual profiles row for a new auth user; see Phase 4 for profiles RLS.';

drop trigger if exists on_auth_user_created on auth.users;
create trigger on_auth_user_created
  after insert on auth.users
  for each row
  execute function public.handle_new_user();
```

### 0.0.289 · `20260929100200_phase1_seed_reference_data.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Generated built-in reference data — 1,442 rows over six editable tables, under the same stable IDs the client seeds — upserted so a customized row is never overwritten.

```sql
-- Phase 1 — Built-in reference data, generated by tool/generate_supabase_seed.dart.
-- Do not edit by hand: change lib/core/constants/pre_seeded_data.dart and re-run
--   dart run tool/generate_supabase_seed.dart
--
-- Every ID here is the stable deterministic tag the Drift seed migrations
-- (Task 04) already use, so a device, the server and a backup all name the same
-- row. Rows are upserted with `on conflict (id) do update ... where
-- is_user_modified = false`, mirroring the client's seed runner: a row the user
-- or firm has customized is never overwritten by a later seed. `version` starts
-- at 1 (the server's insert revision) and re-seeding never bumps it; the
-- `server_updated_at` trigger stamps the pull cursor.
--
-- Table class: all six tables are push/pull domain (editable, firm-shared
-- reference data with user-scoped customizations in individual mode).

insert into public.court_categories (id, name_key, court_level, order_index, division, district, is_custom, is_user_modified, user_id, firm_id, updated_at, version, synced_at, is_deleted, last_modified_by)
values
  ('appellate_division', 'courtCategory_appellateDivision', 'appellate', 0, null, null, false, false, null, null, 1767225600000, 1, null, false, null),
  ('high_court_division', 'courtCategory_highCourtDivision', 'high_court', 1, null, null, false, false, null, null, 1767225600000, 1, null, false, null),
  ('district_sessions_criminal', 'lowerCourtCategory_districtSessionsCriminal', 'lower', 3, null, null, false, false, null, null, 1767225600000, 1, null, false, null),
  ('district_courts_civil', 'lowerCourtCategory_districtCourtsCivil', 'lower', 2, null, null, false, false, null, null, 1767225600000, 1, null, false, null),

on conflict (id) do update set

where court_categories.is_user_modified = false;

on conflict (id) do update set
  court_name = excluded.court_name,
  higher_lower_courts = excluded.higher_lower_courts,
```

## App Version 0.0.290 — Task 16 Phase 2 — Server-Internal Ledgers

### 0.0.290 · `20260929101000_phase2_server_internal_tables.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** The four server-internal ledgers (invite_attempts, mutation_receipts, app_admins, payment_approval_events) with deny-all RLS, two immutability triggers and the rate-limit index.

```sql
-- Phase 2 — Server-internal tables: no client ever touches these directly.
--
-- Table class: server-managed. They are written and read only by the
-- SECURITY DEFINER RPCs added in Phase 3 (and by service-role/migration
-- operations), so RLS is enabled here with **no policies at all**: with RLS on
-- and no policy, `authenticated` and `anon` are denied every command, while the
-- table owner and the service role continue to work. Phase 4 re-asserts
-- `enable row level security` for every table in the schema, which is
-- idempotent.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- invite_attempts — rolling rate-limit ledger for join_firm()
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.invite_attempts (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  attempted_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
comment on table public.invite_attempts is
  'Server-internal. attempted_at is server now() only, never client-supplied; rows older than one day are deleted lazily by join_firm().';

-- The rolling-window count in join_firm() is an index-only lookup.
create index if not exists invite_attempts_user_attempted_at_idx
  on public.invite_attempts (user_id, attempted_at desc);

alter table public.invite_attempts enable row level security;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- mutation_receipts — idempotency ledger for push_changes() (Task 18)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.mutation_receipts (
  mutation_id text primary key,
  actor_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  entity_type text not null,
  entity_id text not null,
  request_hash text not null,
  terminal_status text not null,
  result_payload jsonb not null,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
comment on table public.mutation_receipts is
  'Server-internal. Retain at least 210 days (the maximum supported offline window plus retry buffer), and never purge a receipt still referenced by case_history, conflict_log or an audit record. mutation_id mirrors the client-generated key so a retry returns the original result.';

alter table public.mutation_receipts enable row level security;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- app_admins — immutable admin registry
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.app_admins (
  user_id uuid primary key references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  granted_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  granted_by uuid references auth.users (id)
);
comment on table public.app_admins is
  'Server-internal admin registry. No client policy of any kind; grants and revocations happen only through audited migrations or service-role operations. Ships empty — see supabase/README.md for the bootstrap insert.';

alter table public.app_admins enable row level security;

-- Grant/revoke are INSERT/DELETE by a privileged actor, so only UPDATE is
-- refused: an existing grant is immutable, a revocation stays possible.
create or replace function public.reject_admin_update()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  raise exception 'app_admins rows are immutable; revoke by deleting the row'
    using errcode = '55000';
end;
$$;

drop trigger if exists trg_app_admins_no_update on public.app_admins;
create trigger trg_app_admins_no_update
  before update on public.app_admins
  for each row
  execute function public.reject_admin_update();

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- payment_approval_events — immutable approval audit ledger
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create table if not exists public.payment_approval_events (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  payment_submission_id uuid not null unique
    references public.payment_submissions (id) on delete restrict,
  user_id uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  approved_by uuid not null references auth.users (id),
  plan_type text not null,
  amount integer not null,
  previous_expiry timestamptz,
  new_expiry timestamptz not null,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now()
);
comment on table public.payment_approval_events is
  'One immutable event per submission (payment_submission_id is UNIQUE), written only by approve_payment() in the same transaction as the approval itself. Owners may read their own events, admins all of them; there is no update or delete path.';

alter table public.payment_approval_events enable row level security;

-- An audit ledger with no update/delete path: refuse both, for every role that
-- does not first disable the trigger deliberately.
create or replace function public.reject_ledger_mutation()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  raise exception '% is an immutable audit ledger', TG_TABLE_NAME
    using errcode = '55000';
end;
$$;

drop trigger if exists trg_payment_approval_events_immutable
  on public.payment_approval_events;
create trigger trg_payment_approval_events_immutable
  before update or delete on public.payment_approval_events
  for each row
  execute function public.reject_ledger_mutation();
```

## App Version 0.0.291 — Task 16 Phase 3 — Helpers, Guards, RPCs, Realtime And Column Privileges

### 0.0.291 · `20260929102000_phase3_access_helpers.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Access helpers (1/6): current_firm_id, is_firm_owner, can_edit_firm, can_delete_firm, is_premium, is_admin, can_read_topic and sha256_hex, all SECURITY DEFINER so policies cannot recurse.

```sql
-- Phase 3 (1/6) — Access helpers.
--
-- Every guard, RPC and RLS policy asks the same handful of questions, and none
-- of them may be answered with a plain subquery:
--
--   * `profiles` RLS allows same-firm reads, so a policy on `profiles` that
--     reads `firm_members` and a policy on `firm_members` that reads
--     `profiles` would recurse forever;
--   * the delete-transition trigger must read permissions for a row the caller
--     may not SELECT;
--   * `approve_payment` and `join_firm` must not depend on the caller's own
--     table privileges at all.
--
-- So each question is one SECURITY DEFINER STABLE function: it reads the base
-- tables as the owner (RLS is not FORCEd, so the owner bypasses it) and returns
-- a plain boolean. Entitlement is always DERIVED from `subscription_expiry`
-- plus the 48-hour grace window — never from `subscription_status`, which is
-- display-only and goes stale because nothing fires at the expiry instant.
--
-- EXECUTE stays granted to PUBLIC on purpose: RLS policy expressions run with
-- the *caller's* privileges, so revoking would break the policies that use
-- them.

-- SHA-256 hex of a normalized invite code. join_firm() hashes the input with
-- this, and the client's invite generator (Task 20) must hash the normalized
-- code the same way: trim, uppercase, then sha256 hex.
create or replace function public.sha256_hex(p_input text)
returns text
language sql
immutable
set search_path = pg_catalog, public, extensions
as $$
  -- digest() lives in the schema pgcrypto was installed into (extensions).
  select pg_catalog.encode(digest(p_input, 'sha256'), 'hex');
$$;

comment on function public.sha256_hex(text) is
  'sha256 hex of the input; the single hashing convention for invite codes.';

-- The caller's firm, or null for an individual account.
create or replace function public.current_firm_id()
returns uuid
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select p.firm_id from public.profiles p where p.id = auth.uid();
$$;

-- True when the caller owns the firm.
create or replace function public.is_firm_owner(p_firm_id uuid)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select exists (
    select 1 from public.firms f
     where f.id = p_firm_id and f.owner_id = auth.uid()
  );
$$;

-- True when the caller may edit ordinary fields of a firm-scoped row: the firm
-- owner, or a member whose `can_edit` flag is on. A null firm (individual
-- account) is never firm-scoped, so this is false for it.
create or replace function public.can_edit_firm(p_firm_id uuid)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select p_firm_id is not null
     and (
       exists (select 1 from public.firms f
                where f.id = p_firm_id and f.owner_id = auth.uid())
       or exists (select 1 from public.firm_members m
                   where m.firm_id = p_firm_id
                     and m.user_id = auth.uid()
                     and m.can_edit)
     );
$$;

-- True when the caller may delete (soft-delete) a firm-scoped row.
create or replace function public.can_delete_firm(p_firm_id uuid)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select p_firm_id is not null
     and (
       exists (select 1 from public.firms f
                where f.id = p_firm_id and f.owner_id = auth.uid())
       or exists (select 1 from public.firm_members m
                   where m.firm_id = p_firm_id
                     and m.user_id = auth.uid()
                     and m.can_delete)
     );
$$;

-- Derived premium entitlement: expiry + 48h grace, both server-side instants.
create or replace function public.is_premium(p_user_id uuid)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select exists (
    select 1 from public.profiles p
     where p.id = p_user_id
       and p.subscription_expiry is not null
       and p.subscription_expiry + interval '48 hours' > now()
  );
$$;

comment on function public.is_premium(uuid) is
  'Premium entitlement = subscription_expiry + 48h grace > now(). Never reads subscription_status.';

-- True when the caller is a registered admin (app_admins registry).
create or replace function public.is_admin()
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select exists (
    select 1 from public.app_admins a where a.user_id = auth.uid()
  );
$$;

-- True when the caller may subscribe to a Realtime topic: their own user topic,
-- or their firm's topic. Used by the `realtime.messages` policy only.
create or replace function public.can_read_topic(p_topic text)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select p_topic = 'user:' || coalesce(auth.uid()::text, '')
      or (
        public.current_firm_id() is not null
        and p_topic = 'firm:' || public.current_firm_id()::text
      );
$$;
```

### 0.0.291 · `20260929102100_phase3_sync_and_guard_triggers.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Sync and write guards (2/6): server_updated_at stamping, the delete transition and resurrection rules, the free-tier 50-case ceiling and the payment submission rate limit, on the domain tables only.

```sql
-- Phase 3 (2/6) — Sync columns and the write guards on the push/pull domain.
--
-- Four behaviours, applied to the twelve domain tables only (AGENTS.md rule 4 —
-- the pull-only caches, preference tables and server-managed tables deliberately
-- get none of them):
--
--   1. `server_updated_at` maintenance — the only column a delta pull filters
--      on, so it is trigger-assigned and never client-written.
--   2. Delete transition (`is_deleted false -> true`) — RLS `WITH CHECK` sees
--      only the NEW row and cannot compare OLD to NEW, so the field-level
--      `can_delete` rule has to be a trigger.
--   3. Resurrection (`is_deleted true -> false`) — refused unless a
--      transaction-local authorization flag is set by a separately audited
--      administrator recovery path. The guard never compares client
--      timestamps, client versions or editor UUIDs.
--   4. The two defense-in-depth limits that RLS cannot express: the free-tier
--      50-case ceiling and the 3-payments-per-24h ceiling.
--
-- The sync columns are maintained here; the `push_changes` RPC (Task 18) is
-- responsible for `version` and `last_modified_by`.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1. server_updated_at
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.set_server_updated_at()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  new.server_updated_at := now();
  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.set_server_updated_at() is
  'BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE on every domain table: the delta-pull cursor is server-assigned.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. Delete transition requires can_delete inside a firm
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.enforce_delete_transition()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  if old.is_deleted = false and new.is_deleted = true then
    -- Individual rows (no firm) are the creator's own; a firm row needs either
    -- the owner or a member holding can_delete. An associate without it must go
    -- through the delete-request workflow instead.
    if not public.can_delete_firm(old.firm_id)
       and old.firm_id is not null then
      raise exception 'delete_not_permitted'
        using errcode = '42501',
              hint = 'Requires can_delete in this firm; raise a delete request instead.';
    end if;
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 3. Resurrection guard
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.enforce_no_resurrection()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  if old.is_deleted = true and new.is_deleted = false then
    -- Only a deliberate, audited administrator recovery path may undo a
    -- tombstone, and it marks that intent for the rest of its transaction:
    --   select set_config('app.allow_resurrection', 'true', true);
    if coalesce(current_setting('app.allow_resurrection', true), 'false')
       <> 'true' then
      raise exception 'resurrection_not_permitted'
        using errcode = '42501',
              hint = 'A soft-deleted row is a tombstone; recovery is an audited admin operation.';
    end if;
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.enforce_no_resurrection() is
  'Refuses is_deleted true -> false unless the transaction set app.allow_resurrection. Compares no client timestamps, versions or UUIDs.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4a. Free-tier case ceiling (defense in depth)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.enforce_free_case_limit()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  c_free_case_limit constant integer := 50;
  v_existing integer;
begin
  if public.is_premium(new.user_id) then
    return new;
  end if;

  -- Serialize count-and-insert for this owner: under READ COMMITTED two
  -- concurrent inserts could otherwise both read 49.
  perform pg_catalog.pg_advisory_xact_lock(
    pg_catalog.hashtext(new.user_id::text)
  );

  select pg_catalog.count(*) into v_existing
    from public.cases c
   where c.user_id = new.user_id
     and c.is_deleted = false;

  if v_existing >= c_free_case_limit then
    raise exception 'free_case_limit_reached'
      using errcode = 'P0001',
            hint = 'A free account may hold 50 live cases; the client shows the paywall.';
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.enforce_free_case_limit() is
  'Rejects an insert past 50 live cases on a free (derived) entitlement; soft-deleted cases do not count.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 4b. Payment submission rate limit
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.enforce_payment_rate_limit()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  c_daily_limit constant integer := 3;
  v_recent integer;
begin
  -- Server time only: a client-supplied submitted_at is overwritten, never
  -- trusted, and the rolling count reads this column.
  new.submitted_at := now();

  select pg_catalog.count(*) into v_recent
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.user_id = new.user_id
     and s.submitted_at > now() - interval '24 hours';

  if v_recent >= c_daily_limit then
    raise exception 'payment_submission_limit_reached'
      using errcode = 'P0001',
            hint = 'Three submissions per 24 hours; the client shows the friendly message.';
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Attach the triggers to the twelve domain tables
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

do $$
declare
  v_table text;
  v_tables text[] := array[
    'cases', 'case_history', 'notes', 'case_references', 'court_categories',
    'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types', 'on_behalfs',
    'delete_requests', 'conflict_log'
  ];
begin
  foreach v_table in array v_tables loop
    execute format(
      'drop trigger if exists trg_%1$s_server_updated_at on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create trigger trg_%1$s_server_updated_at before insert or update on '
      'public.%1$I for each row execute function public.set_server_updated_at()',
      v_table
    );

    execute format(
      'drop trigger if exists trg_%1$s_delete_transition on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create trigger trg_%1$s_delete_transition before update on public.%1$I '
      'for each row execute function public.enforce_delete_transition()',
      v_table
    );

    execute format(
      'drop trigger if exists trg_%1$s_resurrection_guard on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create trigger trg_%1$s_resurrection_guard before update on public.%1$I '
      'for each row execute function public.enforce_no_resurrection()',
      v_table
    );
  end loop;
end;
$$;

-- The two limits belong to their own tables and are not part of the loop above:
-- `cases` counts rows per owner, and payment submissions are not a domain table.
drop trigger if exists trg_cases_free_limit on public.cases;
create trigger trg_cases_free_limit
  before insert on public.cases
  for each row
  execute function public.enforce_free_case_limit();

drop trigger if exists trg_payment_submissions_rate_limit
  on public.payment_submissions;
create trigger trg_payment_submissions_rate_limit
  before insert on public.payment_submissions
  for each row
  execute function public.enforce_payment_rate_limit();
```

### 0.0.291 · `20260929102200_phase3_payment_approval.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** approve_payment(uuid) (3/6): admin-only, atomic lock-validate-extend-mirror-audit path, plus mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm().

```sql
-- Phase 3 (3/6) — Payment approval.
--
-- `approve_payment(payment_submission_id uuid)` is the only way a subscription
-- is extended. It is admin-only, entirely server-timed and atomic: lock,
-- validate, extend, mirror to the firm, write exactly one immutable audit
-- event, or roll the whole transaction back.
--
-- `mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm()` is deployed here in the same migration,
-- because it is the only write path for `firms.subscription_expiry` and the RPC
-- depends on it.
--
-- Table class: `payment_submissions` and `payment_approval_events` are
-- server-managed (no client write policy exists for either).

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Owner -> firm subscription mirror
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  -- Only the owner's profile mirrors anywhere; for an associate this matches no
  -- firm and does nothing. Expiry is authoritative, status is display-only.
  update public.firms f
     set subscription_expiry = new.subscription_expiry,
         subscription_status = new.subscription_status
   where f.owner_id = new.id;

  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm() is
  'AFTER UPDATE OF subscription_expiry ON profiles for owner rows only: the single write path for firms.subscription_expiry.';

drop trigger if exists trg_profiles_mirror_owner_subscription on public.profiles;
create trigger trg_profiles_mirror_owner_subscription
  after update of subscription_expiry on public.profiles
  for each row
  when (old.subscription_expiry is distinct from new.subscription_expiry)
  execute function public.mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm();

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- approve_payment
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.approve_payment(payment_submission_id uuid)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- Configured plan prices, in BDT (implementation plan §7). Kept here as named
  -- constants so a price change is one migration, and so the RPC validates the
  -- amount the server believes rather than the amount the client sent.
  c_monthly_bdt constant integer := 100;
  c_annual_bdt constant integer := 1000;

  v_submission public.payment_submissions%rowtype;
  v_profile public.profiles%rowtype;
  v_event public.payment_approval_events%rowtype;
  v_previous_expiry timestamptz;
  v_new_expiry timestamptz;
begin
  -- A GRANT to `authenticated` is not authorization: every caller shares that
  -- database role, so the registry itself decides.
  if not public.is_admin() then
    raise exception 'admin_required' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Missing rows return a status without revealing anything about other users.
  select * into v_submission
    from public.payment_submissions s
   where s.id = payment_submission_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- The parameter has the same name as the column, and `payment_approval_events`
  -- has that column, so the reference must be qualified: unqualified it is
  -- ambiguous and the function fails with "It could refer to either a PL/pgSQL
  -- variable or a table column" the first time it is called.
  select * into v_event
    from public.payment_approval_events e
   where e.payment_submission_id = approve_payment.payment_submission_id;

  -- Idempotent retries: a duplicate call can never extend expiry twice.
  if v_submission.status = 'approved' then
    if found then
      return jsonb_build_object(
        'status', 'already_approved',
        'new_expiry', v_event.new_expiry
      );
    end if;
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'already_resolved');
  end if;

  if v_submission.status <> 'pending' then
    -- A rejected submission needs a separately audited reset; approval is not
    -- a back door around that.
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'already_resolved');
  end if;

  if v_submission.plan_type not in ('monthly', 'annual') then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_plan');
  end if;

  -- The CASE is parenthesized because plpgsql reads an IF condition up to the
  -- first top-level `then`; an unwrapped CASE ends it at its own `when ... then`
  -- and the function then fails to compile with "syntax error at end of input".
  if v_submission.amount <> (case v_submission.plan_type
                               when 'monthly' then c_monthly_bdt
                               else c_annual_bdt
                             end) then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_amount');
  end if;

  select * into v_profile
    from public.profiles p
   where p.id = v_submission.user_id
   for update;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_found');
  end if;

  -- Server time decides the base; a lapsed-but-not-expired window is not lost.
  v_previous_expiry := v_profile.subscription_expiry;
  v_new_expiry := greatest(coalesce(v_previous_expiry, now()), now())
    + case v_submission.plan_type
        when 'monthly' then interval '1 month'
        else interval '1 year'
      end;

  update public.profiles p
     set subscription_expiry = v_new_expiry,
         subscription_status = 'premium'
   where p.id = v_submission.user_id;

  update public.payment_submissions s
     set status = 'approved',
         approved_at = now(),
         approved_by = auth.uid(),
         updated_at = (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint
   where s.id = payment_submission_id;

  -- Exactly one immutable event per submission (UNIQUE on the FK).
  insert into public.payment_approval_events (
    payment_submission_id, user_id, approved_by, plan_type, amount,
    previous_expiry, new_expiry
  )
  values (
    payment_submission_id, v_submission.user_id, auth.uid(),
    v_submission.plan_type, v_submission.amount,
    v_previous_expiry, v_new_expiry
  );

  return jsonb_build_object(
    'status', 'approved',
    'previous_expiry', v_previous_expiry,
    'new_expiry', v_new_expiry
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.approve_payment(uuid) is
  'Admin-only atomic MFS approval: lock, validate, extend entitlement, mirror to the firm, one audit event.';

-- The owner must not be reachable by ordinary clients, and the grant is for the
-- shared `authenticated` role only (the app_admins check above is the real gate).
-- `from public` is not enough on Supabase: its default privileges grant EXECUTE
-- on every new function in `public` *directly* to `anon` and `authenticated`,
-- and a direct grant outlives a revoke aimed at PUBLIC. Verified against the
-- project — `anon` could call this RPC until `anon` was named here.
revoke all on function public.approve_payment(uuid) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.approve_payment(uuid) to authenticated;
```

### 0.0.291 · `20260929102300_phase3_join_firm.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** join_firm(code) (4/6): rate-limited, sha256-normalized invite redemption that creates the membership, relinks the profile and consumes the invite.

```sql
-- Phase 3 (4/6) — join_firm(code).
--
-- Onboarding is online-only and runs entirely here: an associate cannot SELECT
-- `firm_invites` before membership, so redemption has to be a SECURITY DEFINER
-- RPC. One call rate-limits, matches, creates the membership, relinks the
-- profile, consumes the invite — or returns a status without changing anything.
--
-- Two details matter for correctness:
--   * a FAILED match records its attempt through a normal `return`, never by
--     inserting and then raising — a raise would roll the attempt back with the
--     statement and the rate limit would never fill up;
--   * only the sha256 hex of the normalized code is ever compared, so a
--     plaintext code exists only in the user's hands and the invite row.

create or replace function public.join_firm(code text)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  -- Five failures per 15 minutes, and attempts older than a day are dropped by
  -- the call itself, so no scheduled job is needed.
  c_max_failures constant integer := 5;
  c_window constant interval := interval '15 minutes';
  c_retention constant interval := interval '1 day';

  v_user_id uuid := auth.uid();
  v_code_hash text;
  v_invite public.firm_invites%rowtype;
  v_failures integer;
  v_used_at bigint;
begin
  if v_user_id is null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'unauthenticated');
  end if;

  delete from public.invite_attempts a
   where a.attempted_at < now() - c_retention;

  select pg_catalog.count(*) into v_failures
    from public.invite_attempts a
   where a.user_id = v_user_id
     and a.attempted_at > now() - c_window;

  if v_failures >= c_max_failures then
    raise exception 'Too many attempts — try again later'
      using errcode = 'P0001';
  end if;

  -- Normalization: trim, uppercase, sha256 hex. The client's invite generator
  -- (Task 20) must hash a code the same way.
  v_code_hash := public.sha256_hex(upper(pg_catalog.btrim(coalesce(code, ''))));

  select * into v_invite
    from public.firm_invites i
   where i.code_hash = v_code_hash
     and i.used_by is null
     and i.revoked = false
   for update;

  if not found then
    -- Non-exception return path: the attempt insert must commit.
    insert into public.invite_attempts (user_id) values (v_user_id);
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'invalid_code');
  end if;

  v_used_at := (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint;

  -- Single-use means single-use: a retry by someone already in the firm still
  -- consumes the code rather than leaving it redeemable for a stranger.
  if exists (
    select 1 from public.firm_members m
     where m.firm_id = v_invite.firm_id and m.user_id = v_user_id
  ) then
    update public.firm_invites i
       set used_by = v_user_id, used_at = v_used_at
     where i.id = v_invite.id;

    delete from public.invite_attempts a where a.user_id = v_user_id;

    return jsonb_build_object(
      'status', 'already_member',
      'firm_id', v_invite.firm_id
    );
  end if;

  insert into public.firm_members (
    firm_id, user_id, role, can_add, can_edit, can_delete
  )
  values (v_invite.firm_id, v_user_id, 'associate', true, false, false);

  update public.profiles p
     set firm_id = v_invite.firm_id,
         role = 'associate'
   where p.id = v_user_id;

  update public.firm_invites i
     set used_by = v_user_id, used_at = v_used_at
   where i.id = v_invite.id;

  delete from public.invite_attempts a where a.user_id = v_user_id;

  return jsonb_build_object(
    'status', 'joined',
    'firm_id', v_invite.firm_id
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.join_firm(text) is
  'Single-use invite redemption: rate limit, hash, membership, profile relink, invite consumed — one transaction.';

-- `anon` is named explicitly for the same reason as `approve_payment`: a direct
-- EXECUTE grant from Supabase's default privileges outlives a revoke of PUBLIC.
revoke all on function public.join_firm(text) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.join_firm(text) to authenticated;
```

### 0.0.291 · `20260929102400_phase3_realtime_broadcast.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Realtime notification (5/6): one {table, id} Broadcast ping per change to the firm or user topic, with the can_read_topic policy on realtime.messages.

```sql
-- Phase 3 (5/6) — Realtime: notification-only Broadcast from the database.
--
-- The app never subscribes to `postgres_changes` (that ships whole rows and
-- makes Supabase evaluate RLS for every subscriber on every change). Instead a
-- trigger on each domain table sends a tiny `{table, id}` ping to a private
-- topic, and the client answers with a micro delta pull for that one row:
-- `firm:{firm_id}` for firm rows, `user:{user_id}` for individual rows.
--
-- Two project-level prerequisites live in the dashboard, not in SQL:
-- Realtime Authorization must be enabled, and topic access is enforced there
-- through the `realtime.messages` policy below.

create or replace function public.broadcast_row_change()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_row jsonb := to_jsonb(new);
  v_topic text;
begin
  -- Payload is deliberately tiny: the client pulls the row itself, so RLS and
  -- conflict handling stay on the REST path.
  if v_row ->> 'firm_id' is not null then
    v_topic := 'firm:' || (v_row ->> 'firm_id');
  elsif v_row ->> 'user_id' is not null then
    v_topic := 'user:' || (v_row ->> 'user_id');
  elsif v_row ->> 'requested_by' is not null then
    -- delete_requests carries no user_id; the requester is the scoped party.
    v_topic := 'user:' || (v_row ->> 'requested_by');
  else
    -- Nothing to scope the ping to; stay silent rather than broadcast wide.
    return null;
  end if;

  perform realtime.send(
    jsonb_build_object('table', TG_TABLE_NAME, 'id', v_row ->> 'id'),
    'row_change',
    v_topic,
    true
  );

  return null;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.broadcast_row_change() is
  'AFTER INSERT/UPDATE on every domain table: sends {table, id} to the row''s private firm/user topic.';

do $$
declare
  v_table text;
  v_tables text[] := array[
    'cases', 'case_history', 'notes', 'case_references', 'court_categories',
    'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types', 'on_behalfs',
    'delete_requests', 'conflict_log'
  ];
begin
  foreach v_table in array v_tables loop
    execute format(
      'drop trigger if exists trg_%1$s_broadcast on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create trigger trg_%1$s_broadcast after insert or update on public.%1$I '
      'for each row execute function public.broadcast_row_change()',
      v_table
    );
  end loop;
end;
$$;

-- Topic authorization: a client may receive only its own user topic and its
-- firm's topic. Realtime evaluates this policy per subscriber, so the payload
-- above never needs to carry the row itself.
--
-- `realtime.messages` is owned by the Realtime service rather than the
-- migration role, so on a project where the policy cannot be created from SQL
-- the failure is reported and the policy is created from the dashboard instead
-- (see supabase/README.md).
do $$
begin
  execute 'drop policy if exists realtime_messages_read_own_topics '
       || 'on realtime.messages';
  execute $policy$
    create policy realtime_messages_read_own_topics on realtime.messages
      for select to authenticated
      using (public.can_read_topic(realtime.topic()))
  $policy$;
exception
  when insufficient_privilege then
    raise notice
      'Realtime topic policy not created (%). Enable Realtime Authorization and '
      'create realtime_messages_read_own_topics from the dashboard.',
      sqlerrm;
  when undefined_table then
    raise notice
      'realtime.messages is not present on this project (%).',
      sqlerrm;
end;
$$;
```

### 0.0.291 · `20260929102500_phase3_column_privileges.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Column privileges (6/6): REVOKEs so the fields only the server may write — approval state, entitlement, firm ownership — cannot be set by a client even inside a permitted UPDATE.

```sql
-- Phase 3 (6/6) — Column privileges: the fields only the server may write.
--
-- RLS cannot restrict *which columns* an UPDATE touches, so a row policy alone
-- would let its beneficiary write server-authoritative fields. Privileges are
-- the gate, for the shared `authenticated` role and for `anon`.
--
-- A column-level REVOKE alone does not work here, and the first version of this
-- migration proved it against the live project: Supabase grants *table-level*
-- privileges on every table in `public` to `anon` and `authenticated` through
-- its default privileges, and a table-level GRANT is not undone by a
-- column-level REVOKE — `has_column_privilege('authenticated','public.profiles',
-- 'subscription_expiry','UPDATE')` stayed true and `update public.profiles set
-- subscription_expiry = ...` still succeeded as that role.
--
-- The working shape is therefore: drop the table-level UPDATE, then GRANT UPDATE
-- back on the columns a client may legitimately write. Anything not named in a
-- GRANT stays server-only, so a column added by a later migration is closed by
-- default rather than open by default.
--
-- The SECURITY DEFINER RPCs are unaffected: they run as the migration owner,
-- whose own privileges are not touched by a revoke aimed at `authenticated`.

-- Approval state is written only by approve_payment(). A client submits a
-- payment; it never edits one, so it keeps no UPDATE privilege here at all.
revoke update on public.payment_submissions from authenticated, anon;

-- Entitlement and identity are server-managed. `subscription_expiry` and
-- `subscription_status` are the entitlement pair; `role` and `firm_id` are
-- included because RLS cannot stop a user from promoting themselves to owner
-- or relinking themselves to another firm's id, and every policy and guard
-- trusts those two columns. (Deliberate addition beyond the task's literal
-- list — flagged in docs/currentlyWorking.md.) A user may edit their own
-- display name, which is the only self-service write the Phase 4 policy allows.
revoke update on public.profiles from authenticated, anon;
grant update (full_name) on public.profiles to authenticated;

-- The firm's mirrored entitlement, and its owner link, are written by
-- mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm() and by firm administration only. The
-- owner may rename the firm, which is the only write `firms_update_owner`
-- permits that is not server-owned.
revoke update on public.firms from authenticated, anon;
grant update (name) on public.firms to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.292 — Task 16 Phase 4 — Row Level Security

### 0.0.292 · `20260929103000_phase4_rls_core_tables.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** RLS for the case-data half (1/3): cases, case_history, notes, case_references, delete_requests and conflict_log scoped to self or same-firm, with no DELETE policy anywhere.

```sql
-- Phase 4 (1/3) — RLS for the case-data half of the push/pull domain.
--
-- The matrix, verbatim from the plan's Row-Level Security Strategy table:
--
--   cases / case_history  SELECT own or same-firm; INSERT own; UPDATE own or a
--                         firm member with can_edit (coarse — the can_delete
--                         transition is the Phase 3 trigger's job); DELETE
--                         never, because deletion is a soft delete.
--   notes / case_references  same shape.
--   delete_requests       firm members SELECT/INSERT; the requester may edit
--                         their own pending request, a can_delete reviewer may
--                         act on it; never DELETE (audit trail).
--   conflict_log          own rows only; never DELETE.
--
-- RLS `WITH CHECK` sees only the NEW row, so nothing here tries to express the
-- delete transition — see `enforce_delete_transition()`.

alter table public.cases enable row level security;
alter table public.case_history enable row level security;
alter table public.notes enable row level security;
alter table public.case_references enable row level security;
alter table public.delete_requests enable row level security;
alter table public.conflict_log enable row level security;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- cases
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists cases_select_scoped on public.cases;
create policy cases_select_scoped on public.cases
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or firm_id = public.current_firm_id()
  );

drop policy if exists cases_insert_own on public.cases;
create policy cases_insert_own on public.cases
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );

drop policy if exists cases_update_scoped on public.cases;
create policy cases_update_scoped on public.cases
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  )
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  );

-- No DELETE policy on purpose: deletion is `is_deleted = true`.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- case_history (user_id/firm_id are denormalized, so no join is needed)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists case_history_select_scoped on public.case_history;
create policy case_history_select_scoped on public.case_history
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or firm_id = public.current_firm_id()
  );

drop policy if exists case_history_insert_own on public.case_history;
create policy case_history_insert_own on public.case_history
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );

drop policy if exists case_history_update_scoped on public.case_history;
create policy case_history_update_scoped on public.case_history
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  )
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  );

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- notes
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists notes_select_scoped on public.notes;
create policy notes_select_scoped on public.notes
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or firm_id = public.current_firm_id()
  );

drop policy if exists notes_insert_own on public.notes;
create policy notes_insert_own on public.notes
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );

drop policy if exists notes_update_scoped on public.notes;
create policy notes_update_scoped on public.notes
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  )
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  );

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- case_references
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists case_references_select_scoped on public.case_references;
create policy case_references_select_scoped on public.case_references
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or firm_id = public.current_firm_id()
  );

drop policy if exists case_references_insert_own on public.case_references;
create policy case_references_insert_own on public.case_references
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );

drop policy if exists case_references_update_scoped on public.case_references;
create policy case_references_update_scoped on public.case_references
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  )
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)
  );

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- delete_requests
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists delete_requests_select_firm on public.delete_requests;
create policy delete_requests_select_firm on public.delete_requests
  for select to authenticated
  using (firm_id = public.current_firm_id());

drop policy if exists delete_requests_insert_firm on public.delete_requests;
create policy delete_requests_insert_firm on public.delete_requests
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    requested_by = auth.uid()
    and firm_id = public.current_firm_id()
  );

-- The requester edits their own still-pending request; a reviewer with
-- can_delete acts on it. Anything else is refused.
drop policy if exists delete_requests_update_scoped on public.delete_requests;
create policy delete_requests_update_scoped on public.delete_requests
  for update to authenticated
  using (
    (requested_by = auth.uid() and status = 'pending')
    or public.can_delete_firm(firm_id)
  )
  with check (
    requested_by = auth.uid()
    or public.can_delete_firm(firm_id)
  );

-- Never DELETE: the request queue is an audit trail, and a resolved request
-- stays visible after the target is tombstoned.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- conflict_log (own rows only; the mirror exists for cross-device portability)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists conflict_log_select_own on public.conflict_log;
create policy conflict_log_select_own on public.conflict_log
  for select to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists conflict_log_insert_own on public.conflict_log;
create policy conflict_log_insert_own on public.conflict_log
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );

drop policy if exists conflict_log_update_own on public.conflict_log;
create policy conflict_log_update_own on public.conflict_log
  for update to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid())
  with check (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    and (firm_id is null or firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );
```

### 0.0.292 · `20260929103100_phase4_rls_reference_tables.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** RLS for the six editable reference tables (2/3): built-in rows readable by every authenticated user, custom rows scoped to creator or firm, soft delete only.

```sql
-- Phase 4 (2/3) — RLS for the six editable reference tables.
--
-- All six share one matrix (plan, Row-Level Security Strategy + Rev 2.4 §1):
--
--   SELECT  every authenticated user sees the built-in rows; a custom row is
--           visible to its creator and, in firm mode, to the firm;
--   INSERT  only as yourself, scoped to your own firm (or to no firm);
--   UPDATE  the creator, or a firm member with can_edit — which is also how a
--           firm shares a customization, since the edit lands on a row the
--           whole firm can read;
--   DELETE  never: deletion is a soft delete, guarded by the can_delete
--           trigger from Phase 3.
--
-- One consequence worth stating plainly: a *built-in* row has no creator and no
-- firm, so `user_id = auth.uid()` and `can_edit_firm(firm_id)` are both false
-- for it. Editing a pre-seeded court, step, case type, role, category or case
-- category is therefore a local-only act until the client models it as a custom
-- row — flagged in docs/currentlyWorking.md.

alter table public.court_categories enable row level security;
alter table public.case_categories enable row level security;
alter table public.courts enable row level security;
alter table public.steps enable row level security;
alter table public.case_types enable row level security;
alter table public.on_behalfs enable row level security;

do $$
declare
  v_table text;
  v_tables text[] := array[
    'court_categories', 'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types',
    'on_behalfs'
  ];
begin
  foreach v_table in array v_tables loop
    execute format(
      'drop policy if exists %1$s_select_scoped on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create policy %1$s_select_scoped on public.%1$I for select to '
      'authenticated using (is_custom = false or user_id = auth.uid() or '
      'firm_id = public.current_firm_id())',
      v_table
    );

    execute format(
      'drop policy if exists %1$s_insert_own on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create policy %1$s_insert_own on public.%1$I for insert to '
      'authenticated with check (user_id = auth.uid() and (firm_id is null or '
      'firm_id = public.current_firm_id()))',
      v_table
    );

    execute format(
      'drop policy if exists %1$s_update_scoped on public.%1$I',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create policy %1$s_update_scoped on public.%1$I for update to '
      'authenticated using (user_id = auth.uid() or '
      'public.can_edit_firm(firm_id)) with check (user_id = auth.uid() or '
      'public.can_edit_firm(firm_id))',
      v_table
    );
  end loop;
end;
$$;
```

### 0.0.292 · `20260929103200_phase4_rls_account_payment_tables.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** RLS for account, firm, preference and payment tables (3/3), and the closing statement that RLS is enabled on every table in the schema.

```sql
-- Phase 4 (3/3) — RLS for the account, firm, preference and payment tables,
-- and the closing statement of "RLS is enabled on every table".
--
-- Matrix (plan, Row-Level Security Strategy + Rev 2.4 §1):
--
--   profiles          readable by self AND same-firm members (Manage
--                     Associates, and the "edited by [Name]" rendering);
--                     UPDATE self only; no INSERT (the sign-up trigger owns
--                     creation); no DELETE.
--   firms             owner or member SELECT; owner INSERT/UPDATE; no DELETE.
--   firm_members      firm members SELECT; the firm owner INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE
--                     (removing an associate is how access is revoked).
--   firm_invites      owner-only everything; redemption goes exclusively
--                     through join_firm(), which is SECURITY DEFINER and never
--                     needs a client policy.
--   payment_submissions  own SELECT and INSERT (the Phase 3 trigger adds the
--                     rate limit); no ordinary UPDATE — approval is the RPC.
--   payment_approval_events  own receipt, or any event for an admin; no writes.
--   user_preferences / case_notification_preferences  self-only, never
--                     firm-shared, no DELETE.
--
-- The four server-internal tables (invite_attempts, mutation_receipts,
```
