```sql
-- app_admins, plus payment_approval_events' write side) keep RLS enabled with
-- no policy where the plan says so.

alter table public.profiles enable row level security;
alter table public.firms enable row level security;
alter table public.firm_members enable row level security;
alter table public.firm_invites enable row level security;
alter table public.payment_submissions enable row level security;
alter table public.payment_approval_events enable row level security;
alter table public.user_preferences enable row level security;
alter table public.case_notification_preferences enable row level security;

-- Re-asserted here so "RLS is on for every table this task creates" is one
-- statement about one file, not something to reconstruct from three.
alter table public.invite_attempts enable row level security;
alter table public.mutation_receipts enable row level security;
alter table public.app_admins enable row level security;

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- profiles
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists profiles_select_self_or_firm on public.profiles;
create policy profiles_select_self_or_firm on public.profiles
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    id = auth.uid()
    or (firm_id is not null and firm_id = public.current_firm_id())
  );

-- UPDATE self only. Which columns may move is decided by the Phase 3 column
-- revokes (role, firm_id and the entitlement pair are server-owned).
drop policy if exists profiles_update_self on public.profiles;
create policy profiles_update_self on public.profiles
  for update to authenticated
  using (id = auth.uid())
  with check (id = auth.uid());

-- No INSERT policy: `handle_new_user()` creates the row as the owner.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- firms
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists firms_select_owner_or_member on public.firms;
create policy firms_select_owner_or_member on public.firms
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    owner_id = auth.uid()
    or id = public.current_firm_id()
  );

drop policy if exists firms_insert_owner on public.firms;
create policy firms_insert_owner on public.firms
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (owner_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists firms_update_owner on public.firms;
create policy firms_update_owner on public.firms
  for update to authenticated
  using (owner_id = auth.uid())
  with check (owner_id = auth.uid());

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- firm_members
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists firm_members_select_firm on public.firm_members;
create policy firm_members_select_firm on public.firm_members
  for select to authenticated
  using (firm_id = public.current_firm_id());

drop policy if exists firm_members_insert_owner on public.firm_members;
create policy firm_members_insert_owner on public.firm_members
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id));

drop policy if exists firm_members_update_owner on public.firm_members;
create policy firm_members_update_owner on public.firm_members
  for update to authenticated
  using (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id))
  with check (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id));

drop policy if exists firm_members_delete_owner on public.firm_members;
create policy firm_members_delete_owner on public.firm_members
  for delete to authenticated
  using (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id));

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- firm_invites (owner-only; redemption is join_firm(), not table access)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists firm_invites_select_owner on public.firm_invites;
create policy firm_invites_select_owner on public.firm_invites
  for select to authenticated
  using (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id));

drop policy if exists firm_invites_insert_owner on public.firm_invites;
create policy firm_invites_insert_owner on public.firm_invites
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (
    public.is_firm_owner(firm_id)
    and created_by = auth.uid()
  );

drop policy if exists firm_invites_update_owner on public.firm_invites;
create policy firm_invites_update_owner on public.firm_invites
  for update to authenticated
  using (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id))
  with check (public.is_firm_owner(firm_id));

-- No DELETE policy: an unused code is revoked, never removed.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- payment_submissions
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists payment_submissions_select_own on public.payment_submissions;
create policy payment_submissions_select_own on public.payment_submissions
  for select to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists payment_submissions_insert_own on public.payment_submissions;
create policy payment_submissions_insert_own on public.payment_submissions
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (user_id = auth.uid());

-- No UPDATE or DELETE policy: approval is approve_payment() only.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- payment_approval_events
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists payment_approval_events_select_own_or_admin
  on public.payment_approval_events;
create policy payment_approval_events_select_own_or_admin
  on public.payment_approval_events
  for select to authenticated
  using (
    user_id = auth.uid()
    or public.is_admin()
  );

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- per-user preferences (self-only, never firm-shared)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

drop policy if exists user_preferences_select_own on public.user_preferences;
create policy user_preferences_select_own on public.user_preferences
  for select to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists user_preferences_insert_own on public.user_preferences;
create policy user_preferences_insert_own on public.user_preferences
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists user_preferences_update_own on public.user_preferences;
create policy user_preferences_update_own on public.user_preferences
  for update to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid())
  with check (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists case_notification_preferences_select_own
  on public.case_notification_preferences;
create policy case_notification_preferences_select_own
  on public.case_notification_preferences
  for select to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists case_notification_preferences_insert_own
  on public.case_notification_preferences;
create policy case_notification_preferences_insert_own
  on public.case_notification_preferences
  for insert to authenticated
  with check (user_id = auth.uid());

drop policy if exists case_notification_preferences_update_own
  on public.case_notification_preferences;
create policy case_notification_preferences_update_own
  on public.case_notification_preferences
  for update to authenticated
  using (user_id = auth.uid())
  with check (user_id = auth.uid());
```

## App Version 0.0.293 — Task 16 Phase 5 — Indexes And The Tombstone Purge

### 0.0.293 · `20260929104000_phase5_indexes_and_tombstone_purge.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Delta-pull and payment indexes for every scoped cursor read, plus purge_tombstones(180 days) scheduled daily through pg_cron.

```sql
-- Phase 5 — Indexes and the scheduled tombstone purge.
--
-- Two jobs:
--
--   1. Make every read the client performs index-backed. The delta pull is
--      scoped (one firm, or one user) and ordered by the composite cursor
--      `(server_updated_at, id)`, so each domain table gets the unscoped
--      composite for a full pull plus a scoped variant for the normal case.
--   2. Purge tombstones after 180 days — long enough that the 150-day
--      per-device extended-offline rule can never resurrect data (a device
--      coming back inside that window still finds every tombstone it needs),
--      short enough that deleted rows do not accumulate forever.
--
-- Table class: indexes and the purge touch the push/pull domain class only.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1a. Payment submission window index (the rate limiter and the UI both read it)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create index if not exists payment_submissions_user_submitted_at_idx
  on public.payment_submissions (user_id, submitted_at desc);

-- `invite_attempts_user_attempted_at_idx` is created with its table in Phase 2.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 1b. Delta-pull indexes
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

do $$
declare
  v_table text;
  -- Every domain table except delete_requests carries user_id; that one carries
  -- the requester instead and is handled below.
  v_tables text[] := array[
    'cases', 'case_history', 'notes', 'case_references', 'court_categories',
    'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types', 'on_behalfs',
    'conflict_log'
  ];
begin
  foreach v_table in array v_tables loop
    execute format(
      'create index if not exists %1$s_delta_idx on public.%1$I '
      '(server_updated_at, id)',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create index if not exists %1$s_firm_delta_idx on public.%1$I '
      '(firm_id, server_updated_at, id)',
      v_table
    );
    execute format(
      'create index if not exists %1$s_user_delta_idx on public.%1$I '
      '(user_id, server_updated_at, id)',
      v_table
    );
  end loop;
end;
$$;

create index if not exists delete_requests_delta_idx
  on public.delete_requests (server_updated_at, id);
create index if not exists delete_requests_firm_delta_idx
  on public.delete_requests (firm_id, server_updated_at, id);
create index if not exists delete_requests_requester_delta_idx
  on public.delete_requests (requested_by, server_updated_at, id);

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- 2. Tombstone purge
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.purge_tombstones(p_retention_days integer default 180)
returns integer
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_cutoff timestamptz;
  v_table text;
  v_deleted integer;
  v_total integer := 0;
  -- Children before parents: the FK graph is case data -> cases -> the
  -- reference tables that cases point at, with case_categories and
  -- court_categories last. Deleting in this order never trips a foreign key.
  v_order text[] := array[
    'case_history',
    'delete_requests',
    'conflict_log',
    'notes',
    'case_references',
    'cases',
    'case_types',
    'steps',
    'courts',
    'case_categories',
    'on_behalfs',
    'court_categories'
  ];
begin
  -- server_updated_at is stamped by the delete transition itself, so it is the
  -- tombstone's age.
  v_cutoff := now() - make_interval(days => p_retention_days);

  foreach v_table in array v_order loop
    execute format(
      'delete from public.%1$I where is_deleted = true '
      'and server_updated_at < $1',
      v_table
    )
    using v_cutoff;

    get diagnostics v_deleted = row_count;
    v_total := v_total + v_deleted;
  end loop;

  return v_total;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.purge_tombstones(integer) is
  'Deletes soft-deleted rows older than the retention (180 days by default), children first.';

-- Schedule it daily. pg_cron is a project-level extension: where it cannot be
-- created from SQL the function still exists and the schedule is made from the
-- dashboard instead (see supabase/README.md).
do $$
begin
  begin
    create extension if not exists pg_cron;
  exception
    when others then
      raise notice
        'pg_cron could not be created (%); schedule public.purge_tombstones() '
        'from the dashboard.', sqlerrm;
  end;

  if exists (select 1 from pg_extension where extname = 'pg_cron') then
    -- Re-running replaces the job instead of stacking a second one.
    perform cron.unschedule('purge-tombstones')
     where exists (select 1 from cron.job where jobname = 'purge-tombstones');

    perform cron.schedule(
      'purge-tombstones',
      '15 3 * * *',
      'select public.purge_tombstones()'
    );

    raise notice 'pg_cron: purge-tombstones scheduled daily at 03:15.';
  else
    raise notice
      'pg_cron is not installed; public.purge_tombstones() exists but is not '
      'scheduled yet.';
  end if;
end;
$$;
```

## App Version 0.0.300 — Task 16 Audit Closure — The Analytics Column

### 0.0.300 · `20260929105000_profiles_last_successful_sync_at.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** Closes the audit's last column-level gap: adds the plan's `profiles.last_successful_sync_at`, admin analytics only, with a comment stating that the extended-offline reset stays per-device in the local `sync_metadata`.

```sql
-- Audit gap closure — `profiles.last_successful_sync_at` (admin analytics only).
--
-- The plan's `profiles` definition lists this column; the live project never had
-- it, and nothing in Part A reads it. It is added here so the schema matches the
-- plan, and it stays NULL until the sync work of Task 17+ writes it.
--
-- Scope, deliberately narrow:
--
--   * It is NOT the extended-offline reset source. That check is per-*device*:
--     each device keeps its own `last_successful_sync_at` in local storage
--     (`sync_metadata` in Drift) and reads that value on reconnect. A shared
--     server column would be masked by the user's other, daily-syncing device,
--     which is exactly the case the check exists for (plan §3). Do not read this
--     column for that purpose.
--   * There is no Drift mirror. `profiles` is a pull-only cache refreshed from
--     the server, and the device value is local operational state — so the app
--     stores it in `sync_metadata`, not on its cached profile row.
--   * It is not client-writable. Phase 3 revoked table-level UPDATE on
--     `profiles` and granted it back for `full_name` only, so a column added by
--     a later migration is closed by default.
--
-- Table class: server-managed/account (`profiles`). Nothing else is touched.

alter table public.profiles
  add column if not exists last_successful_sync_at timestamptz;

comment on column public.profiles.last_successful_sync_at is
  'Admin analytics only. Never the extended-offline reset source: that check is per-device and reads the local sync_metadata value (plan §3).';
```

## App Version 0.0.305 — Task 17 Phase 5 — Owner Firm Establishment

### 0.0.305 · `20260929110000_phase5_owner_firm_establishment.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** ensure_firm(name) — the idempotent owner-side conversion from individual to firm: creates the owner's firm, relinks `profiles.firm_id`/`role` (both server-owned by the column privileges) and upserts the owner's `firm_members` row, so the invite generator has a firm to invite into.

```sql
-- Task 17 Phase 5 — ensure_firm(name): the one path that turns an individual
-- account into a firm owner.
--
-- Why this is a server function and not a client insert. Revision 2.4's
-- onboarding rule is that an individual "converts to Firm automatically when an
-- associate is added": the owner generates the invite, so the firm has to exist
-- before the code does. The client cannot do it itself, by design:
--   * `20260929102500_phase3_column_privileges.sql` revoked UPDATE on
--     `profiles` from `authenticated` and granted back only `full_name`, so
--     `profiles.role` and `profiles.firm_id` are server-owned — a user cannot
--     promote themselves to owner or relink themselves to another firm's id;
--   * `current_firm_id()` reads `profiles.firm_id` and nothing else, so an owner
--     who merely inserted a `firms` row would still have no firm scope: every
--     firm-scoped policy would ignore them.
-- So the conversion needs SECURITY DEFINER privileges, and that is what this
-- function is.
--
-- Idempotent by design: one firm per owner. Calling it again returns the firm
-- that already exists (renaming it only when a different name is given, which is
-- the same self-service rename `firms_update_owner` + the `firms.name` column
-- grant already allow), and repairs the owner's `firm_members` row rather than
-- creating a second one.
--
-- The new firm starts with the owner's own subscription values, which is the
-- state `mirror_owner_subscription_to_firm()` maintains from then on.

create or replace function public.ensure_firm(p_name text)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_user_id uuid := auth.uid();
  v_now bigint := (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint;
  v_name text := pg_catalog.btrim(coalesce(p_name, ''));
  v_firm public.firms%rowtype;
begin
  if v_user_id is null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'unauthenticated');
  end if;

  -- The name is the UI's, not this function's: a server-side default would be
  -- user-facing copy living outside the ARB, so an empty name is refused.
  if v_name = '' then
    raise exception 'ensure_firm: a firm name is required'
      using errcode = 'P0001';
  end if;

  select * into v_firm
    from public.firms f
   where f.owner_id = v_user_id
   order by f.created_at
   limit 1;

  if not found then
    -- `created_at` is a server instant (`timestamptz`) and takes the column
    -- default: the bigint `v_now` below is epoch ms for the client-facing
    -- `firm_members` stamps only, and the first apply proved the difference by
    -- refusing the value here.
    insert into public.firms (
      name, owner_id, subscription_status, subscription_expiry
    )
    select
      v_name,
      v_user_id,
      p.subscription_status,
      p.subscription_expiry
      from public.profiles p
     where p.id = v_user_id
    returning * into v_firm;

    if not found then
      -- No profile row: the sign-up trigger creates one, so this is a bug state,
      -- never something to explain to a user. Raising here rolls the firm back.
      raise exception 'ensure_firm: no profiles row for the caller'
        using errcode = 'P0001';
    end if;
  elsif v_name is distinct from v_firm.name then
    update public.firms f
       set name = v_name
     where f.id = v_firm.id
    returning * into v_firm;
  end if;

  -- The conversion itself: firm scope for every policy, owner role for the app.
  update public.profiles p
     set firm_id = v_firm.id,
         role = 'owner'
   where p.id = v_user_id;

  -- The owner holds every permission explicitly, so `can_edit_firm` /
  -- `can_delete_firm` and the client's cached flags agree with the role.
  insert into public.firm_members (
    firm_id, user_id, role, can_add, can_edit, can_delete,
    synced_at, permissions_synced_at
  )
  values (
    v_firm.id, v_user_id, 'owner', true, true, true, v_now, v_now
  )
  on conflict (firm_id, user_id) do update
    set role = 'owner',
        can_add = true,
        can_edit = true,
        can_delete = true,
        synced_at = excluded.synced_at,
        permissions_synced_at = excluded.permissions_synced_at;

  return jsonb_build_object(
    'status', 'ready',
    'firm_id', v_firm.id,
    'name', v_firm.name
  );
end;
$$;

comment on function public.ensure_firm(text) is
  'Idempotent owner-side firm establishment: creates the caller''s firm, relinks profiles.firm_id/role and upserts the owner''s firm_members row. The invite generator (and therefore the app) has no other way to reach firm scope.';

-- A direct EXECUTE grant from Supabase's default privileges outlives a revoke of
-- PUBLIC, so `anon` is named explicitly — the same reason join_firm and
-- approve_payment do it.
revoke all on function public.ensure_firm(text) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.ensure_firm(text) to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.307 — Task 17 Audit Follow-Up — Abandoned Onboarding Cleanup

### 0.0.307 · `20260929120000_abandon_provisional_account.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** abandon_provisional_account() — the protected cleanup for an abandoned join-firm onboarding: removes the caller's own Auth account when it is provably provisional (no firm, individual role, no subscription, no authored rows, no firm/payment/admin/sync activity) and refuses with the reason otherwise, so an unfinished sign-up is treated as never created.

```sql
-- Task 17 audit follow-up — abandon_provisional_account(): the protected cleanup
-- that makes an abandoned onboarding "as if never created".
--
-- Why this exists. Task 17's Alignment Addendum asks for exactly this: a failed
-- `join_firm` keeps onboarding retryable, and if the user abandons it before a
-- successful redemption, the provisional profile/Auth account must be removed so
-- it is treated as never created. The client cannot do it — deleting an
-- `auth.users` row is beyond anything `authenticated` may do — so it has to be a
-- SECURITY DEFINER function. The interesting part is not the delete, it is the
-- guard that decides what "provisional" means.
--
-- The guard is deliberately paranoid, because the cost of a wrong deletion is a
-- lawyer's account. Every one of these refuses, and names itself as the reason:
--   * a firm in `profiles.firm_id`, a role other than `individual`, or a
--     subscription (the account has been used for something);
--   * owning a firm, holding a `firm_members` row, or having issued an invite;
--   * any row the account authored — cases, case history, notes, case
--     references, delete requests, conflict records, or custom reference data;
--   * a payment submission or approval, administrator rights, or a sync receipt.
-- Only an account with none of those is removed, which in practice means the one
-- a join-firm sign-up created moments earlier and never redeemed.
--
-- What removal means. `delete from auth.users` cascades to `profiles`,
-- `firm_members`, `user_preferences`, `case_notification_preferences`,
-- `invite_attempts` and `app_admins` — the abandoned account's failed-attempt
-- counter and its per-user preferences go with it, which is what "never created"
-- requires — and every reference that does **not** cascade (`firms.owner_id`,
-- `firm_invites.created_by`, `payment_submissions.user_id`,
-- `payment_approval_events.*`, `mutation_receipts.actor_id` and the domain rows)
-- is one the guard above has already refused. That is why the removal is a
-- single statement that cannot fail on a foreign key.

create or replace function public.abandon_provisional_account()
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_user_id uuid := auth.uid();
  v_reason text;
begin
  if v_user_id is null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'unauthenticated');
  end if;

  -- The profile itself. A missing row is not a provisional account: the sign-up
  -- trigger creates one, so it is a bug state, and nothing is removed.
  select case
           when p.firm_id is not null then 'a firm'
           when p.role <> 'individual' then 'a role'
           when p.subscription_expiry is not null then 'a subscription'
         end
    into v_reason
    from public.profiles p
   where p.id = v_user_id;

  if not found then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_provisional', 'reason', 'no profile');
  end if;

  -- Everything the account may have done. The first table that has a row names
  -- the refusal, so a caller and a test both learn *why* without a second round
  -- trip. Each statement here is either a not-cascading reference or a row whose
  -- loss would be a loss of work.
  if v_reason is null then
    select t.reason
      into v_reason
      from (
        select 'firm ownership' as reason
          from public.firms f where f.owner_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'firm membership'
          from public.firm_members m where m.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'an issued invite'
          from public.firm_invites i where i.created_by = v_user_id
        union all select 'a payment'
          from public.payment_submissions s where s.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'an approval'
          from public.payment_approval_events e
         where e.user_id = v_user_id or e.approved_by = v_user_id
        union all select 'administrator rights'
          from public.app_admins a where a.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'a sync receipt'
          from public.mutation_receipts r where r.actor_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'cases'
          from public.cases c where c.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'case history'
          from public.case_history h where h.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'notes'
          from public.notes n where n.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'case references'
          from public.case_references r where r.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'delete requests'
          from public.delete_requests d
         where d.requested_by = v_user_id or d.reviewed_by = v_user_id
        union all select 'conflict records'
          from public.conflict_log l where l.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'custom courts'
          from public.courts x where x.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'custom court categories'
          from public.court_categories x where x.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'custom case categories'
          from public.case_categories x where x.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'custom steps'
          from public.steps x where x.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'custom case types'
          from public.case_types x where x.user_id = v_user_id
        union all select 'custom on-behalfs'
          from public.on_behalfs x where x.user_id = v_user_id
      ) t
     limit 1;
  end if;

  if v_reason is not null then
    return jsonb_build_object('status', 'not_provisional', 'reason', v_reason);
  end if;

  -- Provisional, and only then: remove the account. See the header for why this
  -- one statement is the whole removal.
  delete from auth.users u where u.id = v_user_id;

  return jsonb_build_object('status', 'removed');
end;
$$;

comment on function public.abandon_provisional_account() is
  'Owner-of-the-account cleanup for an abandoned join-firm onboarding: removes the caller''s own Auth account only when it is provably provisional (no firm, individual role, no subscription, no authored rows, no firm/payment/admin/sync activity), so it is treated as never created. Returns {"status":"removed"}, {"status":"not_provisional","reason":…} or {"status":"unauthenticated"}.';

-- A direct EXECUTE grant from Supabase's default privileges outlives a revoke of
-- PUBLIC, so `anon` is named explicitly — the same reason join_firm, approve_payment
-- and ensure_firm do it.
revoke all on function public.abandon_provisional_account() from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.abandon_provisional_account() to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.310 — Task 17 Follow-Up — The Account Holder's Own Details

### 0.0.310 · `20260929130000_profiles_personal_details.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-29.** The account holder's own details on the profile row: `email` (mirrored from `auth.users` by the sign-up and e-mail-change triggers) plus `bar_association`, `membership_no`, `office_address` and `mobile_no`, granted column-wise for the caller's own row so the Profile screen writes them through PostgREST and a re-install never asks for them again.

```sql
-- Task 17 follow-up — the lawyer's own details live on `profiles`, not only on
-- one device.
--
-- Why this exists. Settings > Accounts & Profile > Profile has always captured
-- full name, bar association, membership number, office address and mobile
-- number, but it kept all five in `SharedPreferences` on that one install. A
-- re-install, a second phone or a cleared app therefore showed five dashes and
-- asked a lawyer to type their own details again. The user asked for the record
-- to live on the account instead.
--
-- `profiles` is a pull-only cache (AGENTS.md rule 4): the server is its writer
-- for `role`, `firm_id`, `subscription_status` and `subscription_expiry`, and
-- none of those ever move from a client. This migration adds the one documented
-- exception the plan already has for `full_name` — the account holder's own
-- details — and nothing else. The Phase 3 column privileges stay exactly as they
-- are for every server-owned column.
--
-- The write path is the boring one. `profiles_update_self` (Phase 4) already
-- restricts a client UPDATE to its own row (`id = auth.uid()`), and
-- `full_name` has been granted column-wise since Phase 3. The four new detail
-- columns join that grant, so the client PATCHes its own row through PostgREST
-- and needs no new function — and no new `SECURITY DEFINER`, which matters
-- because the project's open hardening item is precisely the number of
-- anon-executable definer functions.
--
-- `email` is deliberately **not** granted: it is the account's login identity,
-- owned by `auth.users`, and a client that could write it could lie about who it
-- is to every firm member who can read the profile. Two triggers keep it exact —
-- `handle_new_user()` fills it at creation (and now also reads the Bar
-- Association the sign-up screen collects), and `handle_user_email_change()`
-- follows an e-mail change on the auth record.

alter table public.profiles
  add column if not exists email text not null default '',
  add column if not exists bar_association text not null default '',
  add column if not exists membership_no text not null default '',
  add column if not exists office_address text not null default '',
  add column if not exists mobile_no text not null default '';

comment on column public.profiles.email is
  'The account''s login e-mail, mirrored from auth.users by handle_new_user() and handle_user_email_change(). Server-owned and never client-writable.';
comment on column public.profiles.bar_association is
  'The bar association the account holder is enrolled in. Self-service: the holder may update this column on their own row.';
comment on column public.profiles.membership_no is
  'The account holder''s bar membership/registration number. Self-service: the holder may update this column on their own row.';
comment on column public.profiles.office_address is
  'The account holder''s chamber/office address. Self-service: the holder may update this column on their own row.';
comment on column public.profiles.mobile_no is
  'The account holder''s mobile number without the +880 prefix, matching the editor''s fixed prefix box. Self-service: the holder may update this column on their own row.';

-- Accounts created before this migration have no e-mail on their profile.
update public.profiles p
   set email = coalesce(u.email, '')
  from auth.users u
 where u.id = p.id
   and p.email = '';

-- The account holder's own details join `full_name` as the only client-writable
-- columns. `role`, `firm_id`, the entitlement pair and `email` keep their
-- Phase 3 revoke, so a permitted UPDATE still cannot move them.
grant update (bar_association, membership_no, office_address, mobile_no)
  on public.profiles to authenticated;

-- The sign-up trigger now also fills the e-mail and the Bar Association the
-- sign-up screen collects, so the row is complete the moment the account exists
-- — even for a sign-up that still has to confirm its address, which has no
-- session to push anything with.
--
-- EXECUTE is deliberately NOT revoked from PUBLIC: PostgreSQL checks the EXECUTE
-- privilege of the role whose statement fires a trigger, and sign-up inserts into
-- auth.users run as the auth service role, so revoking would break account
-- creation. Nothing else is reachable through these functions.
create or replace function public.handle_new_user()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  insert into public.profiles (
    id, email, full_name, bar_association, role, firm_id,
    subscription_status, subscription_expiry, created_at
  )
  values (
    new.id,
    coalesce(new.email, ''),
    coalesce(new.raw_user_meta_data ->> 'full_name', ''),
    coalesce(new.raw_user_meta_data ->> 'bar_association', ''),
    'individual',
    null,
    'free',
    null,
    now()
  )
  on conflict (id) do nothing;

  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.handle_new_user() is
  'Creates the individual profiles row for a new auth user, including their e-mail and the Bar Association from the sign-up metadata; see Phase 4 for profiles RLS.';

-- An e-mail change on the auth record is mirrored onto the profile, so the two
-- can never disagree about the identity the row belongs to.
create or replace function public.handle_user_email_change()
returns trigger
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
begin
  update public.profiles
     set email = coalesce(new.email, '')
   where id = new.id;

  return new;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.handle_user_email_change() is
  'Mirrors auth.users.email onto public.profiles.email, so the cached identity is never stale.';

drop trigger if exists on_auth_user_email_changed on auth.users;
create trigger on_auth_user_email_changed
  after update of email on auth.users
  for each row
  when (old.email is distinct from new.email)
  execute function public.handle_user_email_change();
```

## App Version 0.0.311 — Task 18 Phase 1 — The Atomic Push Pipeline

### 0.0.311 · `20260930140000_push_changes.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-30.** `push_changes(rows jsonb)` — the single atomic push pipeline: entity-type/dependency-rank helpers, the firm-aware entitlement derivation and the write re-check, then one transaction with a savepoint per mutation, optimistic concurrency, idempotent `mutation_receipts`, authoritative `case_history` on a changed `next_date`, cascade soft-deletes and one result per mutation_id.

```sql
-- Task 18 Phase 1 — push_changes(rows jsonb): the single atomic push pipeline.
--
-- Every sync write goes through this one RPC instead of direct table upserts,
-- per implementation plan §1.1. One outer transaction, one implicit savepoint
-- per logical mutation (a PL/pgSQL `begin ... exception ... end` block), a
-- deterministic result for exactly one mutation_id in input order, and a
-- receipt per terminal result so a retry can never apply twice.
--
-- Table classes (AGENTS.md rule 4): it writes only the 12 push/pull domain
-- tables and reads/writes the server-managed `mutation_receipts` ledger. The
-- pull-only caches, preference tables and every other server-managed table are
-- deliberately out of reach: `sync_entity_table` maps only the domain set.
--
-- The client's queue already carries the whole contract in snake_case
-- (`mutation_id`, `entity_type`, `entity_id`, `operation`, `base_version`,
-- `base_payload`, `changed_fields`, `payload`, `pre_mutation_payload`, and an
-- optional `provisional_history` for a next_date-changing case edit), so the
-- wire shape and the local columns are the same names.
--
-- History rule (Rev 2.4 Addendum): the RPC creates the authoritative
-- `case_history` event, in the same savepoint as the parent case mutation, only
-- when an accepted case update changed `next_date`. The client stages a
-- provisional preview locally and sends its identity here; a rejected or
-- conflicting mutation creates no normal history row — its attempted payload
-- belongs in `conflict_log`, written by the client.

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Entity-type resolution and dependency rank
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Singular queue entity_type -> the server table it writes.
create or replace function public.sync_entity_table(p_entity_type text)
returns text
language sql
immutable
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select case p_entity_type
    when 'case' then 'cases'
    when 'case_history' then 'case_history'
    when 'note' then 'notes'
    when 'reference' then 'case_references'
    when 'court_category' then 'court_categories'
    when 'case_category' then 'case_categories'
    when 'court' then 'courts'
    when 'step' then 'steps'
    when 'case_type' then 'case_types'
    when 'on_behalf' then 'on_behalfs'
    when 'delete_request' then 'delete_requests'
    when 'conflict_log' then 'conflict_log'
    else null
  end;
$$;

comment on function public.sync_entity_table(text) is
  'Maps a queue entity_type to its push/pull domain table; null for anything outside the class.';

-- FK-safe ordering: the six editable config tables first, then `cases`, then
-- everything that may reference a case or is a leaf. Within a rank the batch's
-- input order is preserved.
create or replace function public.sync_dependency_rank(p_entity_type text)
returns integer
language sql
immutable
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select case public.sync_entity_table(p_entity_type)
    when 'court_categories' then 0
    when 'case_categories' then 0
    when 'courts' then 0
    when 'steps' then 0
    when 'case_types' then 0
    when 'on_behalfs' then 0
    when 'cases' then 1
    when 'case_history' then 2
    when 'notes' then 2
    when 'case_references' then 2
    when 'delete_requests' then 2
    when 'conflict_log' then 2
    else 3
  end;
$$;

comment on function public.sync_dependency_rank(text) is
  'FK-safe push ordering rank: config tables, then cases, then dependents.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Sync entitlement (firm-aware)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- Premium is DERIVED, never stored (plan §6). An individual uses their own
-- expiry; a firm member uses the firm's mirrored expiry — the plan's rule that
-- sync eligibility for a whole firm follows `firms.subscription_expiry`. The
-- same 48-hour grace window applies either way.
create or replace function public.has_sync_entitlement(p_user_id uuid)
returns boolean
language sql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select exists (
    select 1 from public.profiles p
     where p.id = p_user_id
       and (
         (p.subscription_expiry is not null
          and p.subscription_expiry + interval '48 hours' > now())
         or (p.firm_id is not null and exists (
               select 1 from public.firms f
                where f.id = p.firm_id
                  and f.subscription_expiry is not null
                  and f.subscription_expiry + interval '48 hours' > now()))
       )
  );
$$;

comment on function public.has_sync_entitlement(uuid) is
  'Sync entitlement = own or firm subscription_expiry + 48h grace > now(); never reads subscription_status.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- Write permission re-check (RLS cannot run inside a SECURITY DEFINER batch)
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

-- push_changes runs as the owner, so RLS does not apply to it. Each mutation is
-- therefore re-checked explicitly against the *final* row it would write, with
-- the same rules the Phase 4 policies express.
create or replace function public.sync_can_write(
  p_table text,
  p_row jsonb,
  p_operation text
)
returns boolean
language plpgsql
stable
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_actor uuid := auth.uid();
  v_is_reference boolean := p_table in (
    'court_categories', 'case_categories', 'courts', 'steps', 'case_types',
    'on_behalfs'
  );
begin
  if v_actor is null then
    return false;
  end if;

  if p_operation = 'insert' then
    if p_table = 'delete_requests' then
      return coalesce((p_row ->> 'requested_by')::uuid, null) = v_actor
         and coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null) = public.current_firm_id();
    elsif v_is_reference then
      return coalesce((p_row ->> 'is_custom')::boolean, false)
         and coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
         and (
           p_row ->> 'firm_id' is null
           or coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null) = public.current_firm_id()
         );
    else
      return coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
         and (
           p_row ->> 'firm_id' is null
           or coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null) = public.current_firm_id()
         );
    end if;
  end if;

  -- update / delete
  if p_table = 'delete_requests' then
    return (
      coalesce((p_row ->> 'requested_by')::uuid, null) = v_actor
      and (p_row ->> 'status') = 'pending'
    ) or public.can_delete_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null));
  elsif v_is_reference then
    -- A built-in row has no creator and no firm; only a custom row is writable.
    if not coalesce((p_row ->> 'is_custom')::boolean, false) then
      return false;
    end if;
    return coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
        or public.can_edit_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null));
  elsif p_table = 'conflict_log' then
    return coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor;
  else
    if not (
      coalesce((p_row ->> 'user_id')::uuid, null) = v_actor
      or public.can_edit_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null))
    ) then
      return false;
    end if;
    -- The delete transition additionally needs can_delete, mirroring
    -- enforce_delete_transition().
    if p_operation = 'delete' and (p_row ->> 'firm_id') is not null then
      return public.can_delete_firm(coalesce((p_row ->> 'firm_id')::uuid, null));
    end if;
    return true;
  end if;
end;
$$;

comment on function public.sync_can_write(text, jsonb, text) is
  'Explicit write re-check for push_changes: same rules as the Phase 4 RLS policies, evaluated on the row that would be written.';

-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- push_changes
-- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

create or replace function public.push_changes(rows jsonb)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_actor uuid := auth.uid();
  v_entitled boolean;
  v_n integer;
  v_results jsonb[];
  v_order integer[];
  v_idx integer;
  v_row jsonb;
  v_mutation_id text;
  v_entity_type text;
  v_entity_id text;
  v_operation text;
  v_table text;
  v_payload jsonb;
  v_base_version integer;
  v_existing jsonb;
  v_final jsonb;
  v_new_row jsonb;
  v_old_version integer;
  v_ms bigint;
  v_hash text;
  v_receipt public.mutation_receipts%rowtype;
  v_status text;
  v_error_code text;
  v_message text;
  v_result jsonb;
  v_cols text;
  v_vals text;
  v_bad integer;
  v_has_created_at boolean;
  v_prov jsonb;
  v_hist jsonb;
  v_hist_row jsonb;
  v_child jsonb;
  v_children jsonb;
  v_state text;
  v_msg text;
begin
  if v_actor is null then
    raise exception 'unauthenticated' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Batch invariant: the Flutter client always sends one JSON array.
  if rows is null or jsonb_typeof(rows) <> 'array' then
    raise exception 'push_changes: rows must be a JSON array'
      using errcode = '22023';
  end if;

  v_n := jsonb_array_length(rows);
  if v_n = 0 then
    return jsonb_build_object('results', '[]'::jsonb);
  end if;

  v_entitled := public.has_sync_entitlement(v_actor);
  v_ms := (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint;

  -- One slot per submitted row, so the response is exactly one result per
  -- mutation_id in input order even though processing follows dependency order.
  v_results := array_fill(null::jsonb, array[v_n]);

  select array_agg(i order by r, i)
    into v_order
    from (
      select i,
             public.sync_dependency_rank(
               coalesce(rows -> (i - 1) ->> 'entity_type', '')
             ) as r
        from generate_series(1, v_n) as i
    ) s;

  foreach v_idx in array v_order loop
    v_row := rows -> (v_idx - 1);

    -- Reset every per-mutation accumulator so nothing from the previous
    -- iteration can leak into this one's result.
    v_status := null;
    v_error_code := null;
    v_message := null;
    v_existing := null;
    v_final := null;
    v_new_row := null;
    v_children := null;
    v_hist_row := null;

    v_mutation_id := nullif(v_row ->> 'mutation_id', '');
    v_entity_type := nullif(v_row ->> 'entity_type', '');
    v_entity_id := nullif(v_row ->> 'entity_id', '');
    v_operation := nullif(v_row ->> 'operation', '');

    if v_mutation_id is null or v_entity_type is null
       or v_entity_id is null or v_operation is null then
      raise exception
        'push_changes: every row needs mutation_id, entity_type, entity_id and operation'
        using errcode = '22023';
    end if;

    v_table := public.sync_entity_table(v_entity_type);
    v_hash := public.sha256_hex(v_row::text);

    -- Idempotency: a known mutation_id with the same canonical hash returns the
    -- original result and applies nothing; a different hash is refused.
    select * into v_receipt
      from public.mutation_receipts r
     where r.mutation_id = v_mutation_id;
    if found then
      if v_receipt.request_hash = v_hash then
        v_results[v_idx] := v_receipt.result_payload;
      else
        v_results[v_idx] := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'rejected',
          'error_code', 'mutation_id_reused',
          'message', 'This mutation id was already used with a different payload.'
        );
      end if;
      continue;
    end if;

    -- The implicit savepoint for this logical mutation: a permanent failure
    -- rolls back only this block, while a receipt (written after the block) and
    -- every other mutation survive.
    begin
      if not v_entitled then
        v_status := 'rejected';
        v_error_code := 'entitlement_required';
        v_message := 'Online sync requires an active subscription.';
      elsif v_table is null then
        v_status := 'rejected';
        v_error_code := 'unknown_entity';
        v_message := format('Unknown entity type %s.', v_entity_type);
      else
        v_payload := coalesce(v_row -> 'payload', '{}'::jsonb);

        -- A payload key that is not a real column is a client validation error,
        -- never a silent no-op.
        select count(*) into v_bad
          from jsonb_object_keys(v_payload) k
         where not exists (
           select 1
             from pg_catalog.pg_attribute a
             join pg_catalog.pg_class c on c.oid = a.attrelid
             join pg_catalog.pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
            where n.nspname = 'public'
              and c.relname = v_table
              and a.attname = k
              and a.attnum > 0
              and a.attisdropped = false
         );
        if v_bad > 0 then
          raise exception 'unknown_column'
            using errcode = '42703',
                  hint = 'The payload names a column that does not exist on the server table.';
        end if;

        v_base_version := nullif(v_row ->> 'base_version', '')::int;

        if v_operation = 'insert' then
          -- Inserts require no existing row.
          execute format(
            'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
            v_table
          ) into v_existing using v_entity_id;

          if v_existing is not null then
            v_status := 'conflict';
            v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
            v_message := 'A row with this id already exists.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          else
            -- Server-owned columns are never taken from the client; the
            -- server_updated_at trigger stamps its own column.
            v_final := v_payload
              - 'server_updated_at' - 'version' - 'last_modified_by';
            if v_table = 'conflict_log' then
              -- The server column is a timestamptz defaulted to server now();
              -- the client's epoch-ms value must not be cast into it.
              v_final := v_final - 'created_at';
            end if;
            v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object(
              'version', 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
            );
            -- Only a table that actually has `created_at` may receive the
            -- fallback; the six reference tables do not.
            select exists (
              select 1
                from pg_catalog.pg_attribute a
                join pg_catalog.pg_class c on c.oid = a.attrelid
                join pg_catalog.pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
               where n.nspname = 'public'
                 and c.relname = v_table
                 and a.attname = 'created_at'
                 and a.attnum > 0
            ) into v_has_created_at;
            if v_has_created_at and v_final ->> 'created_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('created_at', v_ms);
            end if;
            if v_final ->> 'updated_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('updated_at', v_ms);
            end if;
            -- `insert ... select *` reads NULL for an absent key rather than
            -- the column default, so a soft-delete flag must be explicit.
            if v_final ->> 'is_deleted' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('is_deleted', false);
            end if;

            if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, 'insert') then
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message := 'The caller may not create this row.';
            else
              -- Only the columns the snapshot carries are named, so a column
              -- the payload omitted still gets its own default rather than
              -- NULL (jsonb_populate_record does not apply defaults).
              select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                     string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                into v_cols, v_vals
                from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k;

              execute format(
                'insert into public.%I (%s) select %s from '
                'jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v',
                v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
              ) using v_final;
              execute format(
                'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                v_table
              ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
              v_status := 'accepted';
            end if;
          end if;
        else
          -- Lock the target row first, then decide.
          execute format(
            'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1 for update',
            v_table
          ) into v_existing using v_entity_id;

          if v_existing is null then
            v_status := 'rejected';
            v_error_code := 'not_found';
            v_message := 'The row no longer exists on the server.';
          elsif (v_existing ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean = true
                and v_operation = 'update' then
            v_status := 'tombstone';
            v_error_code := 'tombstone';
            v_message := 'The row was deleted on the server.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          elsif (v_existing ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean = true
                and v_operation = 'delete' then
            v_status := 'already_resolved';
            v_error_code := 'already_resolved';
            v_message := 'The row was already deleted.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          elsif v_base_version is null
                or v_base_version <> (v_existing ->> 'version')::int then
            v_status := 'conflict';
            v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
            v_message := 'The server version changed since this edit was made.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          else
            v_old_version := (v_existing ->> 'version')::int;
            v_final := (v_existing || v_payload)
              - 'server_updated_at' - 'version' - 'last_modified_by';
            if v_table = 'conflict_log' then
              v_final := v_final - 'created_at';
            end if;
            v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object(
              'version', v_old_version + 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
            );
            if v_operation = 'delete' then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('is_deleted', true);
            end if;
            if v_final ->> 'updated_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('updated_at', v_ms);
            end if;

            if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, v_operation) then
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message := 'The caller may not write this row.';
            else
              -- Update every column the final snapshot carries, except
              -- server_updated_at, which the trigger owns.
              -- Both lists are ordered by the same key, so the columns and
              -- their values can never fall out of step.
              select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                     string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                into v_cols, v_vals
                from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k
               where k <> 'server_updated_at';

              execute format(
                'update public.%I t set (%s) = (%s) '
                'from jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v '
                'where t.id = $2',
                v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
              ) using v_final, v_entity_id;

              execute format(
                'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                v_table
              ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
              v_status := 'accepted';
            end if;
          end if;
        end if;

        -- Atomic history and cascade soft-delete, only on an accepted case
        -- mutation.
        if v_status = 'accepted' and v_table = 'cases' then
          if v_operation = 'delete' then
            -- One accepted case delete soft-deletes its children in this same
            -- savepoint; the changed children ride back to the caller.
            v_children := '[]'::jsonb;
            for v_child in execute format(
              'update public.case_history t '
              'set is_deleted = true, '
              '    version = coalesce(t.version, 1) + 1, '
              '    last_modified_by = $1, '
              '    updated_at = $2 '
              'where t.case_id = $3 and t.is_deleted = false '
              'returning to_jsonb(t)'
            ) using v_actor::text, v_ms, v_entity_id
            loop
              v_children := v_children || jsonb_build_array(v_child);
            end loop;
          elsif v_operation = 'update'
                and (v_final ->> 'next_date') is distinct from
                    (v_existing ->> 'next_date') then
            -- The client's provisional preview supplies its identity so the
            -- authoritative event replaces it one-for-one.
            v_prov := v_row -> 'provisional_history';
            if v_prov is null or jsonb_typeof(v_prov) <> 'object' then
              v_prov := '{}'::jsonb;
            end if;

            v_hist := v_prov || jsonb_build_object(
              'id', coalesce(v_prov ->> 'id', gen_random_uuid()::text),
              'case_id', v_entity_id,
              'user_id', v_new_row -> 'user_id',
              'firm_id', v_new_row -> 'firm_id',
              'source_mutation_id',
                coalesce(v_prov ->> 'source_mutation_id', v_mutation_id),
              'created_by', v_actor,
              'base_case_version',
                coalesce(nullif(v_prov ->> 'base_case_version', '')::int,
                         v_old_version),
              'result_case_version', (v_new_row ->> 'version')::int,
              'version', 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text,
              'is_deleted', false,
              'updated_at', v_ms
            );
            -- Nothing the client omitted is invented: the previous values come
            -- from the authoritative base row.
            if v_hist ->> 'hearing_date' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'hearing_date',
                coalesce(nullif(v_existing ->> 'next_date', '')::bigint, v_ms)
              );
            end if;
            if v_hist ->> 'last_step' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'last_step', v_existing -> 'last_step'
              );
            end if;
            if v_hist ->> 'note_result' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'note_result', v_existing -> 'notes_results'
              );
            end if;
            if v_hist ->> 'result' is null then
              v_hist := v_hist || jsonb_build_object(
                'result', v_existing -> 'result'
              );
            end if;

            execute format(
              'insert into public.case_history select * from '
              'jsonb_populate_record(null::public.case_history, $1)'
            ) using v_hist;

            -- Hand the authoritative row back so the client can reconcile its
            -- provisional preview (same id) in one write.
            execute format(
              'select to_jsonb(t) from public.case_history t where t.id = $1'
            ) into v_hist_row using v_hist ->> 'id';
          end if;
        end if;
      end if;

      -- Terminal result for this mutation.
      if v_status = 'accepted' then
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'accepted',
          'version', (v_new_row ->> 'version')::int,
          'row', v_new_row,
          'children', coalesce(v_children, '[]'::jsonb),
          'history', v_hist_row
        );
      elsif v_status in ('conflict', 'tombstone', 'already_resolved') then
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', v_status,
          'version', nullif(v_new_row ->> 'version', '')::int,
          'row', v_new_row,
          'error_code', v_error_code,
          'message', v_message
        );
      else
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'rejected',
          'error_code', coalesce(v_error_code, 'validation_failed'),
          'message', coalesce(v_message, 'The mutation was refused.')
        );
      end if;
    exception
      when others then
        get stacked diagnostics
          v_state = returned_sqlstate,
          v_msg = message_text;
        -- A permanent, per-mutation failure is caught and reported; anything
        -- else is infrastructure and aborts the whole RPC.
        if v_state not in (
          '42501', 'P0001', '23502', '23503', '23505', '23514',
          '22P02', '22007', '22003', '42804', '42703'
        ) then
          raise;
        end if;
        v_result := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'rejected',
          'error_code', case v_state
            when '42501' then 'permission_denied'
            when 'P0001' then 'entitlement_required'
            else 'validation_failed'
          end,
          'message', v_msg
        );
    end;

    -- Receipt for every terminal result, in the same transaction. It is written
    -- after the savepoint block so a per-mutation rejection still records one.
    insert into public.mutation_receipts (
      mutation_id, actor_id, entity_type, entity_id, request_hash,
      terminal_status, result_payload
    ) values (
      v_mutation_id, v_actor, v_entity_type, v_entity_id, v_hash,
      v_result ->> 'status', v_result
    )
    on conflict (mutation_id) do nothing;

    v_results[v_idx] := v_result;
  end loop;

  return jsonb_build_object('results', (
    select coalesce(jsonb_agg(v_results[k] order by k), '[]'::jsonb)
      from generate_subscripts(v_results, 1) as k
  ));
end;
$$;

comment on function public.push_changes(jsonb) is
  'Single atomic push pipeline: optimistic concurrency, per-mutation savepoints, idempotent receipts, authoritative case history and cascade soft-deletes.';

-- The RPC and its two SECURITY DEFINER helpers are reachable by no client role
-- directly: push_changes is the only entry point, and it calls them as owner.
-- `anon` is named explicitly for the same reason as the other RPCs — Supabase's
-- default privileges grant EXECUTE directly to it.
revoke all on function public.push_changes(jsonb) from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.push_changes(jsonb) to authenticated;

revoke all on function public.sync_can_write(text, jsonb, text)
  from public, anon, authenticated;
revoke all on function public.has_sync_entitlement(uuid)
  from public, anon, authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.313 — Task 18 Phase 3 — Server Clock For Skew Detection

### 0.0.313 · `20260930150000_sync_server_time.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-30.** `sync_server_time()` — the server clock as a plain stable SQL function, granted to `authenticated` only, so a successful sync can compare device and server time and record the offset past two minutes.

```sql
-- Task 18 Phase 3 — sync_server_time(): the server's own clock, for skew detection.
--
-- The plan's clock-skew rule compares the device's time against the server's on
-- every successful sync and, past ~2 minutes, records the offset and shows the
-- non-intrusive warning. The delta pull returns rows, not a clock, so this is the
-- one small read the client needs.
--
-- Table class: none — it reads no table, only `now()`. It is granted to
-- `authenticated` alone (Supabase's default privileges would otherwise hand it
-- to `anon` directly), and it is deliberately not SECURITY DEFINER because it
-- touches nothing that would need to bypass RLS.

create or replace function public.sync_server_time()
returns timestamptz
language sql
stable
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
  select now();
$$;

comment on function public.sync_server_time() is
  'The server clock, used by the client to detect and record device clock skew on a successful sync.';

revoke all on function public.sync_server_time() from public, anon;
grant execute on function public.sync_server_time() to authenticated;
```

## App Version 0.0.318 — Task 19 Phase 3 — The Conflict Verdict On The Mirrored Row

### 0.0.318 · `20260930160000_conflict_mergeable_column.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-30.** `conflict_log.mergeable_conflict` — the detecting client's three-way verdict on the conflict, carried on the mirrored row so a second device reads the same answer instead of recomputing one from its own copy. `not null default false` reads an old or absent value as "needs a person".

```sql
-- Task 19 Phase 3 — the three-way conflict verdict travels with the conflict.
--
-- `conflict_log` is mirrored to Supabase so a conflict raised on one device can
-- be reviewed on another: Sync Plan §1 says the log "is queued to its Supabase
-- mirror for portability", and the Addendum repeats that the queue metadata must
-- support `mergeable_conflict` from day one.
--
-- Why a column rather than a computed answer: whether a conflict could be merged
-- depends on the three payloads **as they were at detection time** and on the
-- field coupling the detecting client applied. Recomputing it on a second device
-- from its own copy could disagree, and the reviewed verdict would then differ
-- from the one the queue acted on. The client writes it; the mirror carries it.
--
-- `default false` is the safe direction: an old row, or a payload that omits the
-- key, reads as "needs a person" rather than as "safe to merge".

alter table public.conflict_log
  add column if not exists mergeable_conflict boolean not null default false;

comment on column public.conflict_log.mergeable_conflict is
  'True when the detecting client found the local and server changes disjoint and rebased them automatically; false when they overlap, are semantically coupled, or involve a tombstone, so the conflict needs a person.';

-- The local column is the source of the value; the mirror keeps it in step with
-- every other conflict field the client sends through push_changes.
```

### 0.0.318 · `20260930170000_push_changes_created_at_guard.sql`

> **Created 2026-09-30.** `push_changes` again, with one corrected guard: the insert-time `created_at` fallback now fills only an epoch-ms **integer** column, so `conflict_log`'s server-owned `timestamptz` keeps its own default instead of receiving the client's milliseconds (which raised `22007` and made every conflict mirror a permanent rejection — found by running the smoke test with a `conflict_log` row in it).

```sql
-- push_changes: the `created_at` fallback may only fill an epoch-ms integer
-- column.
--
-- Found by running `supabase/tests/push_changes_smoke_test.sql` with a
-- `conflict_log` row in it (Task 19 Phase 3 added the section), not by reading
-- the function. The insert branch strips the client's `created_at` for
-- `conflict_log` — correct, because the server column is a `timestamptz`
-- defaulted to server `now()` — and then the generic fallback put the client's
-- epoch milliseconds back whenever the table had a `created_at` column at all.
-- `jsonb_populate_record` then cast `1790757617282` into the `timestamptz`, which
-- raises `22007`, which the per-mutation handler turns into a permanent
-- `rejected` result: the conflict mirror could never land, so a conflict raised
-- on one device was never reviewable on another (Sync Plan §1, Task 19 Phase 3
-- item 5).
--
-- The guard now asks for the column's **type** as well as its name. That is the
-- honest condition: the fallback exists to fill a client-facing epoch-ms
-- integer, and a server-owned timestamptz must take its own default. Every
-- other table keeps the behavior it had — the six reference tables have no
-- `created_at`, and the domain tables' is an integer.
--
-- Nothing else in `push_changes` changes; the function is replaced whole because
-- that is the only write path a plpgsql body has.

create or replace function public.push_changes(rows jsonb)
returns jsonb
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = pg_catalog, public
as $$
declare
  v_actor uuid := auth.uid();
  v_entitled boolean;
  v_n integer;
  v_results jsonb[];
  v_order integer[];
  v_idx integer;
  v_row jsonb;
  v_mutation_id text;
  v_entity_type text;
  v_entity_id text;
  v_operation text;
  v_table text;
  v_payload jsonb;
  v_base_version integer;
  v_existing jsonb;
  v_final jsonb;
  v_new_row jsonb;
  v_old_version integer;
  v_ms bigint;
  v_hash text;
  v_receipt public.mutation_receipts%rowtype;
  v_status text;
  v_error_code text;
  v_message text;
  v_result jsonb;
  v_cols text;
  v_vals text;
  v_bad integer;
  v_has_created_at boolean;
  v_prov jsonb;
  v_hist jsonb;
  v_hist_row jsonb;
  v_child jsonb;
  v_children jsonb;
  v_state text;
  v_msg text;
begin
  if v_actor is null then
    raise exception 'unauthenticated' using errcode = '42501';
  end if;

  -- Batch invariant: the Flutter client always sends one JSON array.
  if rows is null or jsonb_typeof(rows) <> 'array' then
    raise exception 'push_changes: rows must be a JSON array'
      using errcode = '22023';
  end if;

  v_n := jsonb_array_length(rows);
  if v_n = 0 then
    return jsonb_build_object('results', '[]'::jsonb);
  end if;

  v_entitled := public.has_sync_entitlement(v_actor);
  v_ms := (extract(epoch from clock_timestamp()) * 1000)::bigint;

  -- One slot per submitted row, so the response is exactly one result per
  -- mutation_id in input order even though processing follows dependency order.
  v_results := array_fill(null::jsonb, array[v_n]);

  select array_agg(i order by r, i)
    into v_order
    from (
      select i,
             public.sync_dependency_rank(
               coalesce(rows -> (i - 1) ->> 'entity_type', '')
             ) as r
        from generate_series(1, v_n) as i
    ) s;

  foreach v_idx in array v_order loop
    v_row := rows -> (v_idx - 1);

    -- Reset every per-mutation accumulator so nothing from the previous
    -- iteration can leak into this one's result.
    v_status := null;
    v_error_code := null;
    v_message := null;
    v_existing := null;
    v_final := null;
    v_new_row := null;
    v_children := null;
    v_hist_row := null;

    v_mutation_id := nullif(v_row ->> 'mutation_id', '');
    v_entity_type := nullif(v_row ->> 'entity_type', '');
    v_entity_id := nullif(v_row ->> 'entity_id', '');
    v_operation := nullif(v_row ->> 'operation', '');

    if v_mutation_id is null or v_entity_type is null
       or v_entity_id is null or v_operation is null then
      raise exception
        'push_changes: every row needs mutation_id, entity_type, entity_id and operation'
        using errcode = '22023';
    end if;

    v_table := public.sync_entity_table(v_entity_type);
    v_hash := public.sha256_hex(v_row::text);

    -- Idempotency: a known mutation_id with the same canonical hash returns the
    -- original result and applies nothing; a different hash is refused.
    select * into v_receipt
      from public.mutation_receipts r
     where r.mutation_id = v_mutation_id;
    if found then
      if v_receipt.request_hash = v_hash then
        v_results[v_idx] := v_receipt.result_payload;
      else
        v_results[v_idx] := jsonb_build_object(
          'mutation_id', v_mutation_id,
          'entity_type', v_entity_type,
          'entity_id', v_entity_id,
          'operation', v_operation,
          'status', 'rejected',
          'error_code', 'mutation_id_reused',
          'message', 'This mutation id was already used with a different payload.'
        );
      end if;
      continue;
    end if;

    -- The implicit savepoint for this logical mutation: a permanent failure
    -- rolls back only this block, while a receipt (written after the block) and
    -- every other mutation survive.
    begin
      if not v_entitled then
        v_status := 'rejected';
        v_error_code := 'entitlement_required';
        v_message := 'Online sync requires an active subscription.';
      elsif v_table is null then
        v_status := 'rejected';
        v_error_code := 'unknown_entity';
        v_message := format('Unknown entity type %s.', v_entity_type);
      else
        v_payload := coalesce(v_row -> 'payload', '{}'::jsonb);

        -- A payload key that is not a real column is a client validation error,
        -- never a silent no-op.
        select count(*) into v_bad
          from jsonb_object_keys(v_payload) k
         where not exists (
           select 1
             from pg_catalog.pg_attribute a
             join pg_catalog.pg_class c on c.oid = a.attrelid
             join pg_catalog.pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
            where n.nspname = 'public'
              and c.relname = v_table
              and a.attname = k
              and a.attnum > 0
              and a.attisdropped = false
         );
        if v_bad > 0 then
          raise exception 'unknown_column'
            using errcode = '42703',
                  hint = 'The payload names a column that does not exist on the server table.';
        end if;

        v_base_version := nullif(v_row ->> 'base_version', '')::int;

        if v_operation = 'insert' then
          -- Inserts require no existing row.
          execute format(
            'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
            v_table
          ) into v_existing using v_entity_id;

          if v_existing is not null then
            v_status := 'conflict';
            v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
            v_message := 'A row with this id already exists.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          else
            -- Server-owned columns are never taken from the client; the
            -- server_updated_at trigger stamps its own column.
            v_final := v_payload
              - 'server_updated_at' - 'version' - 'last_modified_by';
            if v_table = 'conflict_log' then
              -- The server column is a timestamptz defaulted to server now();
              -- the client's epoch-ms value must not be cast into it.
              v_final := v_final - 'created_at';
            end if;
            v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object(
              'version', 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
            );
            -- Only a table whose `created_at` is an epoch-ms *integer* column may
            -- receive the fallback: the six reference tables have none, and
            -- `conflict_log.created_at` is a server-owned timestamptz that must
            -- take its own default rather than the client's milliseconds.
            select exists (
              select 1
                from pg_catalog.pg_attribute a
                join pg_catalog.pg_class c on c.oid = a.attrelid
                join pg_catalog.pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
               where n.nspname = 'public'
                 and c.relname = v_table
                 and a.attname = 'created_at'
                 and a.attnum > 0
                 and a.atttypid in ('int8'::regtype, 'int4'::regtype)
            ) into v_has_created_at;
            if v_has_created_at and v_final ->> 'created_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('created_at', v_ms);
            end if;
            if v_final ->> 'updated_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('updated_at', v_ms);
            end if;
            -- `insert ... select *` reads NULL for an absent key rather than
            -- the column default, so a soft-delete flag must be explicit.
            if v_final ->> 'is_deleted' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('is_deleted', false);
            end if;

            if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, 'insert') then
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message := 'The caller may not create this row.';
            else
              -- Only the columns the snapshot carries are named, so a column
              -- the payload omitted still gets its own default rather than
              -- NULL (jsonb_populate_record does not apply defaults).
              select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                     string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                into v_cols, v_vals
                from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k;

              execute format(
                'insert into public.%I (%s) select %s from '
                'jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v',
                v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
              ) using v_final;
              execute format(
                'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1',
                v_table
              ) into v_new_row using v_entity_id;
              v_status := 'accepted';
            end if;
          end if;
        else
          -- Lock the target row first, then decide.
          execute format(
            'select to_jsonb(t) from public.%I t where t.id = $1 for update',
            v_table
          ) into v_existing using v_entity_id;

          if v_existing is null then
            v_status := 'rejected';
            v_error_code := 'not_found';
            v_message := 'The row no longer exists on the server.';
          elsif (v_existing ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean = true
                and v_operation = 'update' then
            v_status := 'tombstone';
            v_error_code := 'tombstone';
            v_message := 'The row was deleted on the server.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          elsif (v_existing ->> 'is_deleted')::boolean = true
                and v_operation = 'delete' then
            v_status := 'already_resolved';
            v_error_code := 'already_resolved';
            v_message := 'The row was already deleted.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          elsif v_base_version is null
                or v_base_version <> (v_existing ->> 'version')::int then
            v_status := 'conflict';
            v_error_code := 'version_conflict';
            v_message := 'The server version changed since this edit was made.';
            v_new_row := v_existing;
          else
            v_old_version := (v_existing ->> 'version')::int;
            v_final := (v_existing || v_payload)
              - 'server_updated_at' - 'version' - 'last_modified_by';
            if v_table = 'conflict_log' then
              v_final := v_final - 'created_at';
            end if;
            v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object(
              'version', v_old_version + 1,
              'last_modified_by', v_actor::text
            );
            if v_operation = 'delete' then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('is_deleted', true);
            end if;
            if v_final ->> 'updated_at' is null then
              v_final := v_final || jsonb_build_object('updated_at', v_ms);
            end if;

            if not public.sync_can_write(v_table, v_final, v_operation) then
              v_status := 'rejected';
              v_error_code := 'permission_denied';
              v_message := 'The caller may not write this row.';
            else
              -- Update every column the final snapshot carries, except
              -- server_updated_at, which the trigger owns.
              -- Both lists are ordered by the same key, so the columns and
              -- their values can never fall out of step.
              select string_agg(quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k),
                     string_agg('v.' || quote_ident(k), ', ' order by k)
                into v_cols, v_vals
                from jsonb_object_keys(v_final) k
               where k <> 'server_updated_at';

              execute format(
                'update public.%I t set (%s) = (%s) '
                'from jsonb_populate_record(null::public.%I, $1) v '
                'where t.id = $2',
                v_table, v_cols, v_vals, v_table
              ) using v_final, v_entity_id;

              execute format(
```
